Al Rayan Dhikr Ring 2.0 · alrayanrings.net · rewrite round 2026-10-12

Eleven ads, rewritten

For each ad, a critic, a rewriter and a final editor worked in turn. Before writing, all three read the full market research and the verified Quran and hadith list. They also read the store pages and looked at the creative. The images and videos below are unchanged from the first round. The copy and video scripts are new, and each ad says whether its creative needs a re-render.

ad_01 copy — FINAL (ship-ready, pending owner confirms

Primary text paste-ready

One lamp still on. A few more SubhanAllah.
Al Rayan Dhikr Ring 2.0: tap to count, silent tracking, a gentle vibration at 33, 66 and 99.

The Prophet ﷺ said (narrated by Aisha, may Allah be pleased with her): "...the most beloved deed to Allah is the most regular and constant even if it were little." (Sahih al-Bukhari 6464)

A big dhikr week, then a quiet one: it happens, and a big week is welcome. What lasts is whatever is easy to pick up again tomorrow.

So the ring is built to make tomorrow easy: prayer-time alerts for the five daily prayers, and your days in the Al Rayan app. [CONFIRM: the Al Rayan app is a real branded app in the app stores]

Counting on fingers remains the Sunnah. The ring is a helper when the count gets long.

$49.99. Free US shipping. 60-day money-back guarantee.
Start small.

Meta headlines

  1. Small dhikr. Every day. On your hand. (37) ← recommended
  2. A few SubhanAllah. Every night. (31)
  3. Free US shipping. 60-day money-back. (36)

Meta description · CTA

Vibration at 33, 66, 99. Prayer-time alerts. (44)

  • Button: Shop Now
  • Line: "Start small." (last line of the primary text)
  • Offer: "$49.99. Free US shipping. 60-day money-back guarantee." No sale price, no "% off".
  • Risk reversal: 60-day money-back guarantee. 1-year tech warranty omitted for length.

Editor notes: changes, checks, re-render, owner to confirm

Editor notes

What I changed from the rewrite, and why

  1. "Lights off" removed from the hook. The picture shows a lit lamp, so "Lights off. A few more SubhanAllah." contradicted the creative. New H1: "One lamp still on. A few more SubhanAllah." (matches the lamp, the mug and the hand). Also updated the "re-render" and "fallback" notes to say so.
  2. "We hear it often" cut (hook H3 and body). It asserts customer feedback we cannot verify. Replaced with a plain observation, "A big dhikr week, then a quiet one: it happens", which keeps the burst-then-quiet idea with no claim and no shame. Body now reads as one reassurance sentence plus the gloss.
  3. "you feel a nudge at each of the five prayers" changed to "prayer-time alerts for the five daily prayers". "Feel a nudge" implies a vibration on prayer alerts, which the allowed claims do not say. "Prayer-time alerts" is the allowed wording. "Your days are waiting in the app" became "your days in the Al Rayan app" (less of a claim about stored data). [CONFIRM] tag on the app kept.
  4. Product line merged and tightened. The two separate "keeps the count on your hand" and "the count is already on it" statements repeated each other. Now one line: "tap to count, silent tracking, a gentle vibration at 33, 66 and 99." Dropped "keeps the count on your hand" from the body (it sounded like the ring stores your count across interruptions, which is unconfirmed).
  5. Close. "Try it for 60 days." changed to "Start small." Ad_02, ad_05 and ad_11 all close on "try it for 60 days"; "Start small." is on-angle and unique. Meta headline 3 changed from "Try it 60 days. Money back." to "Free US shipping. 60-day money-back." (36) so it also differs from ad_09's "60 days, money back" headline and the CTA line.
  6. Meta description shortened from about 61 characters to 44 ("Vibration at 33, 66, 99. Prayer-time alerts.").
  7. H4 hook rewritten so the only quoted words are an exact substring of Bukhari 6464 ("the most regular and constant even if it were little"), with the Prophet ﷺ named outside the quote. H6 reworded to the allowed "prayer-time alerts" wording ("nudges you" dropped).
  8. Word counts rechecked (135 body words without the tag, 149 with it). Headline counts: 37, 31, 36. All at or under 40. On-image caption 17 characters.

Verified, no change needed

  • Hadith: "...the most beloved deed to Allah is the most regular and constant even if it were little." is an exact substring of the verified Bukhari 6464 wording (offer_brief row 13). Attribution is "The Prophet ﷺ said (narrated by Aisha, may Allah be pleased with her)", with a leading ellipsis. Not Tirmidhi 3583 or Muslim 783.
  • Every critique MUST FIX is resolved: (1) attribution fixed; (2) first-person testimonial gone, no "I" owns the ring; (3) logic hole closed by using prayer-time alerts and app history, the consistency features; (4) product and mechanism inside the first 125 characters; (5) no "Your dhikr..." forms and no claim about the viewer's religion or faults; (6) copy no longer fights the display, plus a re-render option (0033).
  • No wudu line, no index-finger direction (ring finger in the picture, no finger named in copy), no rose-gold claim, no store metrics, no star ratings, no sale price, no "waterproof", no "completely silent", no competitors, no superlatives, no riya' count.
  • 33, 66, 99 stated as vibration points only; no implication of a 100th mark and no reward tied to the ring. Sunnah finger-counting line kept and the ring is not ranked above it.

Overlap with the other rewrites (checked against ad_02 to ad_11)

  • ad_08 also quotes Bukhari 6464 with the same excerpt and attribution. ad_01 is the consistency angle and owns this hadith. Recommend ad_08 drop it (it still has Quran 62:10) or the two ads will show the same quote to the same audience.
  • "Try it for 60 days" is used by ad_02, ad_05 and ad_11. Not used here.
  • "Tap to count / gentle vibration at 33, 66, 99" is shared product-fact copy across the set; kept in different sentence shapes. Hook "One lamp still on", headline "Small dhikr. Every day. On your hand." and "Start small." appear in no other ad. ad_11 says "worn on your hand" in body copy only, not as a headline.

Re-render needed (recommended, not blocking)

  • Ring display 5332 to 0033; add the small native caption "Tonight, just 33." (upper left, thin white sans). Keep ring finger, lamp lit, dark phone. Not blocking: without it, the ad still runs (hook sits in the primary text).

Owner to confirm

  • [CONFIRM: the Al Rayan app is a real branded app in the app stores] (primary text, "your days in the Al Rayan app"). Delete the whole app clause if not confirmed.
  • Open sunnah.com/bukhari:6464 once before running, as the offer brief asks for all verified lines.
  • Landing page, not the ad (flagged by the critique; my grep of the current rebuilt product.html / index.html did not find these strings, so re-check the live version): "Most people wear it on the index finger" in the Wear step or FAQ would conflict with the men's-finger rule (this ad's picture shows the ring finger). "8,200+ wearers", star ratings, named testimonials and any "Save $-49.99" label would contradict the no-metrics and no-fabricated-testimonial rules. Fix before sending traffic.
Critique of the previous version

ad_01 — Copy critique (round: rewrite)

Ad: IMAGE 9:16 · ANG7 "consistency over intensity" · A1 · story-confession · lo-fi night bedside (swipe: Yaqeen Books 126182379) Critiqued file: 03-ads/ad_01/copy_final.md (v2 chiefed). Creative viewed: report_media/ad_01_main.jpg, ad_01_img_v1.jpg, ad_01_img_v2.jpg, plus full-res crops of 04-assets/ad_01/images/img_main_a2.png, img_v1.png, img_v2.png. Read in full before writing: CONTEXT.md, intake, user market research, avatar sheet, master avatar research, necessary beliefs, offer brief, brief.md, copy_final.md, chief notes v1/v2, _assign.json + concept_slate entries, all 10 other ads' hooks/primary text/headlines, rebuilt product.html and index.html.

0. Verdict in five lines

  • The structure (confession, failed fixes, hadith, reveal, soft Sunnah line, offer) is clean and calm, and the Sunnah line and no-metrics discipline are good.
  • It is not safe to run as written. It breaks two explicit rules: the Bukhari 6464 attribution ("Narrated by Aisha: ...") and the first-person story that reads as a customer testimonial for the ring.
  • The argument has a logic hole. The hook says "big week, then nothing" (a burnout/consistency collapse). The proof is two single-night lost-count scenes, and the ring is sold as a counter, not as a habit tool. The angle says "consistency", but the copy never uses the two features that actually serve consistency: prayer-time alerts and app history.
  • The 9:16 image has no text. The hook lives only in the primary text, and the headline field repeats it word for word. In Stories/Reels the hook is mostly invisible.
  • The "blame your tools / friction / phone / beads / lost count" middle is the same message that ad_02, 04, 05, 07, 09 and 10 run. This ad's one unique asset (consistency + Bukhari 6464 + night) is under-used.

1. Hook strength (first line / first second)

The image carries no text, so the hook is whatever shows first in the feed: the first ~125 characters of primary text ("I did a big dhikr week. Then nothing. / Not because I stopped caring. / It was late. I was in bed. I wanted to say SubhanAllah a few more...") plus the Meta headline. In Stories/Reels the headline is not shown on the creative at all, and the primary text is truncated. If the first 125 characters do not carry the hook and the product, the ad has no hook there.

Ranking of the five existing hooks for an A1 (and, secondarily, A2) scroller:

Rank Hook Verdict
1 H1 "I did a big dhikr week. Then nothing." Best fit to angle, image and swipe. Two-beat rhythm, a recognisable pattern (a burst, then a drop). Weaknesses: "big dhikr week" is vague, with no scene or number to picture; it is first-person persona (see section 3); it is mildly boast-adjacent ("I did a big dhikr week"); and it promises a habit-collapse story that the body never delivers. 3/5 for scroll-stop.
2 H5 "Why does my dhikr stop after day three?" More specific ("day three") and a self-diagnosis question. Weakness: "my dhikr stop" frames failure, and it asks a question the body answers with friction, not with "day three". Likely stronger than H1 on curiosity.
3 H2 "I lost my count every single night." The best VOC/product tie (Q29) and a concrete night scene that matches the image. But "every single night" is exaggeration, it is first-person, and it is a lost-count hook, not a consistency hook. It would mean changing the angle.
4 H4 "If your dhikr dies by Wednesday, read this." "Wednesday" is the best craft detail of the five. But "If your dhikr dies..." asserts the viewer practises dhikr and frames a failing practice (Meta personal-attributes borderline, and "dies" leans to guilt). Do not use as written.
5 H3 "Your dhikr was never weak. Your tools were." A sound big idea, and it is the store headline. It is already in ad_09 H3 ("Your dhikr was never the problem. Your tools were.") and ad_10 body ("Your dhikr was never weak. Your tools made it hard."), and it asserts the viewer's religious practice in second person. Highest overlap risk.

Honest read: none of the five is a scene hook. Everything the avatar recognises (a bedside phone, a thumb on a ring at night, a tasbih app left open) is in the body, not the first line. A1 would register "dhikr" as the identity flag and keep scrolling unless the first line contains a specific moment.

2. Angle and avatar fit, awareness, beliefs

  • Avatar. The voice is A1-appropriate: tribe vocabulary (SubhanAllah, dhikr, intention), no shame, a Sunnah-safe line. But the scene is "bed at night", while the avatar sheet's A1 pains are workplace, commute and kids. The night scene is a legitimate micro-moment (A1 plus the A1c Tahajjud adjacency), but it is not what A1 is mostly buying for.
  • Angle logic gap (the main structural problem). ANG7 is "small constant beats big burst". The copy runs: 1. A burst then nothing (L1). 2. Evidence = two single-night failures: the phone-to-feed slide (L4) and the lost count on beads (L5). 3. Hadith about constancy (L7-8). 4. "So it was never my intention. It was the friction" (L9). 5. Ring puts the count on your finger (L10). Step 2 does not explain step 1. A lost count on one night does not cause a week-long collapse. And what the hadith recommends (do less, but constantly) is not what the ring does (it counts). The ring's real consistency mechanisms are prayer-time alerts (A-3) and app history/habit tracking (A-4). Neither is mentioned. As written, a sceptical A1 (the avatar sheet says researchers, stage 3-4) will think: "the hadith says do a little each day. Why do I need a ring?"
  • B1 then B2 order. The brief asks for B1 (small and constant matters) then B2 (friction not intention). The copy actually runs B2-evidence (L4-5), then B1 (L7-8, the hadith), then B2 conclusion (L9). That arc works as a story. But the B1 hadith sits at line 7, behind "See more", and A1 already believes B1, so it is the least valuable thing to spend the premium lines on. B3 (Sunnah) is handled well at L11. B4 (silent, no look) is not used at all, even though the bedside scene is the ideal "silent tracking" scene (A-11).
  • Awareness/sophistication. Problem-aware with a stage 3-4 market needs a specific mechanism and moment early. The mechanism (tap, pulse at 33/66/99) arrives at line 10 of 13, about 100 words in. For a Stories/Reels thumb that is never read. The story is also longer than the avatar's patience for a $49.99 impulse, with a 9-line setup and a 3-line close.
  • Message match with the store. The rebuilt home page says "Struggling to stay consistent? Blame the tools, not your heart." and the ad's "it was never my intention" pivot matches that well. Keep that continuity. The store's three-step "Wear / Tap / Review" and "Your history in the app" support a habit story that the ad never tells.

3. Rule violations (every one, with the offending line)

# Rule (CONTEXT) Offending line Verdict
V1 Attribution (Bukhari 6464 is the Prophet's saying narrated by Aisha) L7: Then I read this. Narrated by Aisha: "the most beloved deed to Allah is the most regular and constant even if it were little." (Sahih al-Bukhari 6464) VIOLATION, and the exact error the rules call out. It reads as Aisha's own words. The Prophet ﷺ is never named, there is no ﷺ, and no "(RA)" after Aisha. The quote also starts lowercase mid-sentence ("the most beloved...") with no ellipsis; the verified wording opens "Do good deeds properly, sincerely and moderately and know that your deeds will not make you enter Paradise, and that the most beloved deed...". Fix: "The Prophet ﷺ said (narrated by Aisha): '... the most beloved deed to Allah is the most regular and constant even if it were little.' (Sahih al-Bukhari 6464)" with a leading ellipsis.
V2 Fabricated-testimonial risk L1-L9: the whole first-person story ("I did a big dhikr week", "I reached for my phone", "Another night I used beads", "I lost my count", "Then I read this", "So it was never my intention"), then immediately L10 "Al Rayan Dhikr Ring 2.0 puts the count on your finger." VIOLATION under the new rules. The round-1 chief accepted this as "obviously persona-styled", but CONTEXT overrides it: a first-person story in primary text reads as a customer endorsement. The pivot "So it was never my intention. It was the friction" followed by the ring implies the narrator solved it with the ring. The creative makes it worse: a native, lo-fi photo of a man's hand wearing the ring is the "I" in the story, which is a UGC-style implied customer post (the swipe intentionally reads like a friend's post). The story must move to brand voice ("We hear this a lot..."), second-person scenario ("You do a big dhikr week. Then nothing."), or the founder line only, with no invented details.
V3 Personal-attributes policy Hooks H3 "Your dhikr was never weak." and H4 "If your dhikr dies by Wednesday, read this." (alt hooks, not the primary text) BORDERLINE, do not carry forward. Second-person plus religious practice, with a negative self-perception tone in H4. The primary text itself is safe because it is first person (but see V2), and no line says "Muslim". Prefer situation framing: "Lost your count?", "After a long day, the count stays on your finger."
V4 Riya' H1/L1 "I did a big dhikr week." and the ring display "5332" in the image LOW-MEDIUM. No count is quoted in the text, which is good. But narrating "my big dhikr week" is a first-person announcement of worship, and the image shows a 4-digit total (see section 4). Dhikr is private; the rewrite should not have a character displaying a count or a big week.
V5 Faith accuracy / interpretation L8: Little. Not big once. Small, every day. SHOULD FIX. "Every day" is the gloss on "regular and constant", so it is not in the hadith. That is acceptable if it stays clearly outside the quote. But "Not big once" can read as belittling a big dhikr session, which is not what the hadith says (the hadith is about constancy, not a ban on volume). Say what the dhikr earns, never that the ring does: the copy is clean on that count.
V6 Unconfirmed / [CONFIRM]-class claim L10: No phone to open. and the story setup around "reached for my phone to open the counter" LOW. It leans toward "works without your phone", which is on the [CONFIRM] list (the rebuilt store says yes, the owner has not confirmed). Either drop it, or tag: No phone to open. [CONFIRM: works without phone]. Same if the rewrite leans on the Al Rayan app: [CONFIRM: Al Rayan app is a real branded app in the stores].
V7 Wudu fiqh none in this ad Clean. Do not add a wudu line in the rewrite (and do not say "never take it off").
V8 Men's finger Copy: "puts the count on your finger" names no finger. Image (main, v1, v2): the ring is on the ring finger of a man's hand (checked at full resolution). Clean. Keep it. Flag for the owner: the rebuilt FAQ and "Wear" step say "Most people wear it on the index finger", which conflicts with the men's-finger rule. An ad that sends traffic there needs the landing page fixed, even though this ad doesn't say it.
V9 Rose gold = alloy colour not used Clean.
V10 Store metrics / stars / sale price / superlatives / competitors none Clean. (The rebuilt store pages carry "8,200+ wearers", "4.9" and named testimonials. That is a funnel problem for the owner, not an ad line, but a click from this ad lands on those claims.)
V11 Faith claims about the ring none Clean. L11 "Counting on fingers remains the Sunnah. The ring is a helper when the count gets long." honours the Sunnah and does not rank the ring above it.
V12 Muslim 596a 33+33+34 "A gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99" Clean. It does not imply a 100th mark. If the rewrite ties the 33 pulse to post-salah tasbih, keep the 33/33/34 sum explicit.

Also note for the rewrite (not a violation): "pulse" and "gentle vibration" are both fine; prefer "vibration" for consistency with the store's brand wording where space allows.

4. Copy-to-creative mismatch

What the image actually shows (ad_01_main.jpg): a warm, quiet nightstand; a man's left hand resting flat on the wood; the black ring on the ring finger with a lit, sharp display reading 5332; a dark phone at left edge with a white charging cable; a mug of tea; a glasses case; a green throw; a lamp at the upper right. No bed is visible, no tapping, no text, no tasbih app, no beads.

  • "Then nothing." vs "5332". The line that opens the ad says nothing happened. The hero object shows a count of five thousand three hundred thirty-two. Visually the ad says "a lot happened". The angle is "small and constant" (hadith: "even if it were little"), and the display shows a large total. This reads like a brag counter (riya'-adjacent) and fights the message. Fix in copy by not claiming "small numbers" as the visual, or in the render by making the display read a small count (for example 0033).
  • "I was in bed" / "reached for my phone". The image shows a still hand on a nightstand with the phone dark and charging, no bed, and a cup of tea. It reads "late evening, quiet", not "the dhikr dying". Acceptable as a mood image, but it shows no moment of the story. No beads and no feed appear.
  • The ring is the hero, but nine lines of copy don't mention it. A viewer sees a ring picture, then a story about phones and beads, and meets the product at line 10. The image sets up "product ad" and the text "diary". They only meet at line 10.
  • A male "I". The hand is clearly a man's (hair, build). The text voice is neutral, and the founder line is a woman. It is fine for A1 (mixed gender), but it adds to the implied-testimonial read (V2) and sits oddly with ads 06-08 (women's voices). A hand with no strong gender cues, or a clearly brand-owned framing, would be safer.
  • On-image text quality. None on the image, apart from the ring's own display (clean at full resolution in img_main_a2; at the 603 px report size the "5" can read as "S"). That is a deliberate swipe mirror, but the cost is a hook-less 9:16 image (section 1). No overlay changes are strictly required. If the owner is willing to re-render, one small native-looking text line would pay for itself (see "rerender" in the directions).
  • Variant check (flag only). img_v2 (kitchen mug) shows a clear bottle with amber liquid on the counter at right. In an Islamic ad that can read as alcohol. Do not use v2 unless the bottle is removed. v1 and main are clean. In all three the ring is on the ring finger.

5. Clarity, rhythm, flab, clichés, repetition, overlap

Good rhythm: L1-L2 ("I did a big dhikr week. Then nothing. / Not because I stopped caring."), L8 ("Little. Not big once. Small, every day.").

Flab, clichés and awkward lines (quote and fix direction): - L3 It was late. I was in bed. I wanted to say SubhanAllah a few more times. Three short sentences of scene-setting; the third is the only one that carries tribe emotion. Compress. - L4 Next thing I knew I was deep in my feed, and the dhikr was gone. "Next thing I knew" and "deep in my feed" are stock phrases. "The dhikr was gone" is odd (the intention wasn't gone). Also this is ad_04's entire concept (phone-to-feed trap) and ad_08 H3. - L5 Another night I used beads. A message came in. I lost my count. This is ad_05/ad_07/ad_09/ad_10's concept (lost count on beads). - L6 I wondered if my intention was the weak part. "The weak part" is stilted. - L7 Then I read this. Stock transition. Where was it read? - L9 It was the friction: the phone, the beads, the lost count. "Friction" is a copywriter's word, not avatar vocabulary (the avatar says "lost my count", "pulled into social media"; the store says "tools"). The triple is the same summary line as ad_09 and ad_10. - L10 puts the count on your finger Fine, but "on your finger" sits close to the finger-counting Sunnah line L11 and could muddle the two ("count on a finger" vs "count on the ring"). Say "on the ring" or "on your hand" if keeping both. - L12-13 It is $49.99, with free US shipping and a 60-day money-back guarantee. Tap Shop Now and try it. "It is" and "Tap Shop Now" are padding; the button already says Shop Now. - Meta headline 1 is identical to primary-text line 1. In feed both are visible, so the same sentence appears twice. A wasted headline slot. - Meta description 60-day money-back guarantee repeats Meta headline 3 and L12. - Meta headline 2 Small dhikr. Every day. On your finger. (39 chars) is the strongest headline here. It is clean, on-angle and within the 40 limit. Keep the idea.

Overlap with the other 10 ads: | Element in ad_01 | Also in | |---|---| | Phone-opens-to-feed scene (L4) | ad_04 (whole concept), ad_08 H3 | | Lost count on beads (L5) | ad_05, ad_09 H4, ad_10 H2, ad_07 (same story in first person, same testimonial problem) | | "Never my intention / it was the tools" (H3, L9) | ad_09 H3, ad_10 body, ad_05 "never the problem", ad_04 H4 "It's not your intention. It's the phone." | | Bukhari 6464 | ad_07 (also quotes it, also with mixed attribution) | | "Tap to count. A gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99" | ad_02, 03, 04, 05, 10, 11 | | Sunnah finger line (L11) | ad_07 | | "Tap Shop Now and try it" | ad_02, 05, 09 (near-identical closers) |

What only ad_01 can own: consistency and habit over time (Bukhari 6464), the end-of-day quiet scene, and the honest "a little each day" promise with no guilt. The rewrite should push into those and out of the shared friction triple.

6. Prioritised fix list

MUST FIX

  1. Hadith attribution (V1). Rewrite as "The Prophet ﷺ said (narrated by Aisha, may Allah be pleased with her): '... the most beloved deed to Allah is the most regular and constant even if it were little.' (Sahih al-Bukhari 6464)". Use a leading ellipsis, since the quote is a mid-sentence extract. Keep the wording exactly as in the offer brief table.
  2. Remove the implied customer testimonial (V2). Move the story out of first person: brand voice ("We hear this a lot: ..."), second-person scenario, or the founder line only. Make sure no line after the story reads as "and then the ring fixed it for me". Also let the image be framed as the brand's own picture, not a customer's post.
  3. Fix the logic hole. The cause named in the body (friction) has to be what actually produced the hook's "then nothing". Either change the hook to a lost-count / phone moment, or change the body so the ring answers the burst-then-nothing problem with a consistency feature (prayer-time alerts, app history, a small daily count that is easy to keep).
  4. Hook must work in the first ~125 characters with the product named early. In 9:16 placements the headline and image text do not carry it. Bring the ring and one mechanism line into the first third; trim the setup from 9 lines to about 4.
  5. Drop "Your dhikr..." / "If your dhikr dies..." forms (V3). Use situation framing or brand/first-person scenario voice.
  6. Do not let copy and image fight on count size. Either avoid "little / small numbers" language beside the 5332 image, or request a re-render with a small display count (see rerender note).

SHOULD FIX

  1. Stop duplicating the hook as the Meta headline. Use the hook as line 1 and a different benefit/offer headline (a version of "Small dhikr. Every day. On your finger." is the best of the existing three).
  2. Replace "friction" with avatar/store language ("lost count", "tools", "pulled in") and drop the shared "phone, beads, lost count" triple. Pick one failure at most, and pick the one no other ad leads with.
  3. Add the unused consistency features (A-3 prayer-time alerts, A-4 app history) and, for the bedside scene, A-11 "silent tracking". Tag the app and standalone claims with [CONFIRM] if used (V6).
  4. Rework L8: keep "Little." but remove "Not big once" and say what the hadith says (regular and constant) without belittling a big dhikr session.
  5. Cut stock phrases: "Next thing I knew", "deep in my feed", "Then I read this", "the weak part".
  6. Tighten the close: one offer line, one CTA, no "It is" and no "Tap Shop Now" if the button already says it. Make the Meta description add something other than the guarantee already in the headline.
  7. Avoid the riya' tone: no "my big week", no displayed totals as a boast.

KEEP (what works)

  • The calm, no-guilt voice and the opening beat "Not because I stopped caring."
  • The best rhythm lines: "Then nothing." and "Little. Not big once. Small, every day." (reworded per #10).
  • The Sunnah honour line (L11), which respects finger counting and does not rank the ring above it.
  • No store metrics, no stars, no sale price, no "waterproof", no "completely silent", no competitor names.
  • The offer facts exactly as allowed ($49.99, free US shipping, 60-day money-back).
  • Meta headline idea 2 "Small dhikr. Every day. On your finger." (39 chars).
  • The ring on the ring finger in the image (men's-finger safe), the lit display, the dark phone, the warm bedside mood.
  • The continuity with the store's "Blame the tools, not your heart" section: the pivot idea is good, it just must not repeat the other ads' wording.

7. Directions for the rewrite (3 to 5)

  1. Brand-voice confession, not a customer story. Open with "We hear it a lot: a big dhikr week, then nothing." (or the second-person "You do a big dhikr week. Then nothing."), and make the very next lines a reframe: the habit that lasts is the small one. Keep it clearly the brand talking about a common pattern, not a person who bought the ring. Same hook idea, no testimonial risk.
  2. Make consistency real with the ring's habit features. Let the ring answer "small and constant" with what it does on a normal day: a count that waits on the ring, a gentle vibration at 33, 66 and 99, prayer-time alerts for the five prayers, and history in the app so a small day still shows up. Tag the app (and any phone-free claim) with [CONFIRM]. This closes the logic hole and gives the ad its own territory, away from the shared friction pitch.
  3. One failure scene, not three, and not the ones other ads own. If a scene is needed, make it a late-night small ask ("just a few more SubhanAllah before sleep") and a quiet fix, not the phone-feed or bead-drop concepts. Use A-11 "silent tracking" for the bedside moment. Do not add a bedtime-dhikr hadith (Bukhari 6464 and Tirmidhi 3375 are the only verified lines that fit; anything else is not in the verified table).
  4. Fix the hook for the placement. Put a specific moment and the product in the first ~125 characters so the Stories/Reels truncation still shows the idea. Use a different Meta headline from the primary-text line 1; keep "Small dhikr. Every day." as the headline concept.
  5. Optional re-render (say so if you use it). (a) Display reads a small count (for example 0033) so the picture agrees with "little" and with the 33 pulse. (b) One small native-looking line on the image carrying the hook, since the swipe's zero-text design leaves 9:16 placements with no visible hook. If neither is done, the copy must not contradict the 5332 display and must lead with the primary-text hook. Do not use img_v2 unless the amber bottle on the counter is removed.

Quality gates for whoever rewrites or fixes this: exact hadith wording, Prophet ﷺ named, Aisha as narrator; no first-person product experience; no [CONFIRM]-class claim without a tag; Meta headline at most 40 characters and not a copy of line 1; one CTA; plain, warm, tribe-correct words (dhikr, tasbih, SubhanAllah, salah).

Editor notes: changes, checks, re-render, owner to confirm

Editor notes

What I changed from the rewrite, and why

  1. Put "wudu" back into the ad. The rewrite never said the word anywhere in the copy, so the tribe cue was only "sleeves up". The on-image payoff is now "Wudu splashes are fine." and the primary text says "wudu splashes are fine". This is the exact register CONTEXT allows ("IP67 water resistant, so wudu splashes are fine"). It describes the product, not the viewer, so no personal-attribute risk. It is also the only angle ANG11 has to differentiate it from the other ads.
  2. Removed "stays on your finger" and "as you normally would". "Stays on your finger" sat right next to "slide it aside" and contradicted it. "As you normally would" asserted the viewer's practice (borderline personal-attribute framing). Replaced with a plain instruction: "Slide it aside so water reaches the skin beneath." That matches the fiqh guidance in CONTEXT (move or adjust so water reaches the skin) and never implies wudu is done with the ring untouched.
  3. Named the product category inside the first ~111 characters (H3 content folded into line 2: "A smart tasbih ring on the finger"). The rewrite's first two lines had no product word, which loses colder viewers before "See more". Line 2 also now literally matches the re-rendered split (phone and beads on the left, ring on the right).
  4. Cut "Something always gets put down." It was a soft universal claim and the weakest line. The concrete list does the work.
  5. Tightened the mechanism line. "Then it's back to what it's for: tap to count, and a gentle pulse..." became "Tap to count. A gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99 tells you where you are." The pulse now has a job, and nothing implies the 100th (Muslim 596a is 33+33+34).
  6. One CTA, one offer line. Dropped the repeated "try it for 60 days" (it appeared in the offer line and the CTA). Offer line stays once; CTA is "Tap Shop Now."
  7. Headline 3 and description changed to avoid colliding with other rewrites: "60 days to try it" echoed ad_05 headline 2 ("Built to be kept. 60 days to try.") and ad_09 H4 ("60 days, money back"). Headline 3 is now "$49.99 · 1-year tech warranty"; description carries shipping plus the guarantee so every offer fact appears but not 4 times.
  8. On-image badge 3 "60-DAY GUARANTEE" → "60-DAY MONEY-BACK" (clearer, matches the offer wording) and badge 2 uses "PULSE AT 33 · 66 · 99" (singular, grammatical). Word counts recounted: on-image 23, primary text 82.
  9. Removed commentary (why-recommended paragraphs, change tables, re-render prose) from the copy sections; re-render spec moved here.
  10. Kept from the rewrite: H1 as the lead hook, the sink scene, IP67 discipline (no "waterproof", no submersion), no testimonial, no store metrics, no reward or iman claim, no office or lost-count drift, no VOC-22 lift.

Critique of the previous version

ad_02 (image, ANG11 wudu-ready) — Copy critique

Role: copy critic. Current copy = 03-ads/ad_02/copy_final.md. Creative reviewed: report_media/ad_02_main.jpg, ad_02_img_v2.jpg, 04-assets/ad_02/images/img_main.png, img_v1.png, plus the swipe (Tayyib lip balm) and the rebuilt store pages.

0. One-paragraph verdict

The ad is clean, honest on the product and well-built on the swipe's structure, but it is a weak argument. It sells a feature everyone in the category already has (IP67, Stage 3-4 sophistication, so a copied mechanism) and it invents a pain that does not hold up: "Set a counter down at the sink and you lose your count." A clicker keeps its count, a bead string keeps your place in your hand, so the A1 reader will not recognise this problem, and a fiqh-literate comment section will say so. The hook addresses the viewer as someone who does wudu five times a day, which is a Meta personal-attributes risk. The "split contrast" has no contrast: both panels show the same ring. The ring's display digits are garbled in the render. It needs a truer friction, a situation-first hook, fiqh-safe wudu wording and a re-render of the image.

1. Hook strength (would A1/A2 stop scrolling?)

Current ranking (best to worst) for a problem-aware A1 on mobile:

  1. H1 "Wudu five times a day. Your count needs this." Best of the five because it is a ritual-fact pattern interrupt, and A1 reads it in under a second. Three problems: - "Your count needs this" is a payoff with no stated problem. What does the count need? Water protection? A scroller has to decode "this", and the image does not answer it (see section 4). - It tells the viewer what they do ("Wudu five times a day") and is written to "your". See section 3 (personal attributes). - "Wudu five times a day" is itself a loose claim. Wudu stays valid until broken, so many people make wudu fewer or more than five times. "Five prayers" is the safe fact.
  2. H3 "Lost your count at the sink again?" The most specific scene and the best emotional hook, but "again" implies a repeated failure on the viewer (negative self-perception framing, mild) and the scene is not credible: nobody loses the count at the sink because of the tool. It stays alive only as a scene ("the sink") with a different problem.
  3. H5 / headline "Wear it to wudu. Keep your count." Clear benefit, zero tension, no curiosity. It is also the Meta headline AND the CTA line AND the image v1 headline, so it is spent three times. As a headline it is fine. As a hook it is a benefit statement to someone who has not yet felt a problem.
  4. H2 "2-3 counters in your bag. None made for wudu." Lifts VOC-01 almost word for word ("2-3 manual counters... none are waterproof"), asserts what the viewer owns, and "none made for wudu" is an overreach (beads and finger counting are perfectly fine at wudu). Flabby rhythm.
  5. H4 "Wudu didn't break your count. Your counter did." Worst. In this audience "break" next to "wudu" reads as nullifying wudu, so it confuses on first read. It blames "your counter", an attack on the viewer's own tool. The premise (anyone thinks wudu breaks the count) is untrue. Nothing in the logic holds.

Net: no existing hook is both a pattern interrupt and true to A1's lived friction. The rewrite needs a new primary hook (see directions).

2. Angle, avatar fit, awareness/sophistication, belief order

  • Angle fit. "Wudu-ready" is a real, concrete A1 worry (VOC-01 "none are waterproof"; conversion barrier "water damage during wudu"). It is a objection-answering angle (B5), not a desire angle. Running it as the whole ad means we open on the fear of the product dying, not on the benefit of continuous dhikr. That suits product-aware buyers more than the "problem-aware" label in the brief says.
  • Sophistication. Stage 3-4 means copied mechanisms are dead on arrival. IP67 is on iQibla, Hadiyah and the store pages of the clones. "Water resistant" alone will not differentiate. The only differentiating move is a specific moment (offer_brief: "a specific usage mechanism and moment"). The sink is that moment, but the copy does not use it: it says "wudu" and then jumps to the product spec.
  • Beliefs. The header says "B4 -> B5" but the body installs B5 first (IP67 / wear it to wudu), then B4 (tap, pulse, silent tracking), and the assignment lists B5, B4. The doc is inconsistent with itself. More important, B5 (built well enough) is proven only by one spec word, and B4 (silent, no phone, no look) arrives as a pile of features, one of which ("discreet tool you can have all the time at work", "silent tracking") belongs to the office angle (ad_06/ad_09/ad_11), not the wudu angle. The primary text drifts from sink to desk.
  • Missing, and cheap to add: the fiqh-aware beat (what actually happens at wudu), and the one-line permission/Sunnah safety (not necessary on every ad, but this audience reads fiqh in comments; see section 3).

3. Rule violations and risks (quote the line)

A. Meta personal attributes / identity framing (MUST) - Hook, on-image headline, primary text line 1 and Meta-style framing: "Wudu five times a day. Your count needs this." Second-person plus a stated religious practice. CONTEXT classes this as borderline, with the preference for situation or product framing. This is the ad's main hook and its on-image headline, so the risk is concentrated on the one line that cannot be hidden. Also "Wudu five times a day. 2-3 counters in your bag." asserts both a practice and ownership of tools. - copy_final.md Avatar line "practising Muslim 20-40" and the spine "A practising Muslim who wudus five times a day and carries 2-3 counters... blames himself when the count breaks" are internal only. Nothing must leak, but note the spine itself builds a "blames himself" negative-self-perception frame, which must not be ported into any line. - H3 "Lost your count at the sink again?" Mild: "again" implies a recurring personal failing.

B. Truth / credibility (MUST) - "Set a counter down at the sink and you lose your count." Factually weak. A mechanical clicker keeps its count when set down. A counter does not reset because it touched a sink. The only true version: you stop, your hands get busy, and resuming from memory is where the number blurs (that is the real Q29 interruption pain, and it is not a wudu-specific one). A fiqh-literate commenter will point out that nothing about wudu "loses" a bead count. - "2-3 counters in your bag. None made for wudu." Overreach, see H2. - "Wudu didn't break your count. Your counter did." Untrue premise, see H4. - Spec honesty is fine: "IP67 water resistance", no "waterproof", no shower or swim wording anywhere. Keep that.

C. Wudu fiqh (SHOULD, near-MUST) - "Wear it to wudu." (headline, hook H5, primary text twice, CTA line, image v1). CONTEXT allows "can stay on through wudu splashes" and forbids "never take it off" or implying wudu is complete with the ring untouched. The bare imperative "Wear it to wudu" plus "Keep your count" plus the ring shown under running water, untouched, in the left panel, reads as "ring stays on, wudu done". Scholarly guidance is to move or adjust the ring so water reaches the skin beneath (required by some if it is tight). The rebuilt store FAQ gets this right: "washing your hands and splashes during wudu are fine." The ad should borrow that precise register ("splashes", "water resistant") and never state or show a completed wudu with the ring untouched. - Repeating the phrase 5 times (see C below) makes the absolute reading worse.

D. Faith content - No Quran or hadith is used. That is safe. No attribution errors, no reward-by-ring claim, nothing about the Sunnah of fingers (not required here). Clean. - Quran 62:10 was optional in the brief. Not needed. Do not bolt it on just to have faith content: "remember Allah often" next to a wudu hook adds nothing.

E. Men's finger (MUST CHECK, creative) - Copy is silent on finger, which is correct. The image hand (left panel) is visibly a man's hand and forearm (hair, build), and the ring sits on a finger in the middle of the hand. From the render it is not clear whether this is the ring finger, and the swipe/prompt says "ring finger". A human must confirm on the full-res frame; if it reads as middle or index it must be re-rendered. The rebuilt store also says "Most people wear it on the index finger", which clashes with the CONTEXT rule if a viewer lands from this ad, so the ad should not show a man's index finger.

F. Riya', store metrics, banned claims, FTC - No counts, no bragging: pass. No store metrics, stars or review counts: pass. No superlatives, no competitor naming: pass. No testimonial, no first-person story: pass (this is a strength vs ad_01/ad_07). No sale price or % off: pass. "Rose gold" not used: pass. - "A discreet tool you can have all the time at work." is VOC-22 (possibly paraphrased, from one Reddit poster) written as the brand's own promise. CONTEXT says use VOC as the avatar's language, not attributed; this is close to verbatim and also off-angle. Rewrite, do not keep. - "Silent tracking." is brand wording, allowed. Do not upgrade to "completely silent". It is also not what the ad is about. - "Tap to count. Feel a gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99." allowed. Does not imply the 100th; fine. - The store rebuild includes first-person customer review text (for example about charging twice a week). That is outside this ad, but tell the owner: anyone landing from this ad will see it, and the research has no verified customer quotes.

G. Repetition inside the ad - "Wear it to wudu" appears: Meta headline 1, hook H5, primary text line "Wear it to wudu.", CTA line "wear it to wudu", image v1. Offer facts (60-day guarantee, free US shipping) appear in the badges, the primary text, headline 3 and the description (4 times). Headline 2 "Dhikr ring with IP67 water rating" is a dull spec line and duplicates badge 1.

4. Copy-to-creative mismatch

  • No contrast in a "split_contrast" layout. Swipe logic: left = the problem (cracked lips), right = the product. Here left = hand with the ring on, right = the same ring. There is no problem panel, so "Your count needs this." has no "without this" to land against. The viewer sees two pictures of the solution.
  • "Your count" is not visible. The ring on the left hand is tiny with an unreadable display ("EES 5"-style garble in ad_02_main.jpg; "5332" readable only in img_v2/img_main). The right-hand hero ring's display renders as stacked or vertical digits (QA already flagged "vertical digits" in the run log and kept it as best attempt). The ad is about the count and the count is the one thing that is unreadable. This is the strongest re-render reason.
  • Water on the ring is untouched. The left panel shows water pouring over the back of the hand with the ring in place, no ring adjustment, which is the wudu-fiqh point in C. A hand mid-wudu shows rubbing between the fingers and the ring moved, which is also a more believable picture.
  • Text hierarchy. The black caps line and the green serif line read well. The green line is the largest and the one most skipped ("needs this" is the payoff of a thought that has not been stated). Badge row: two of four badges are offer facts (guarantee, shipping) that also sit in the primary text; "TAP TO COUNT" is the least ownable product fact on the page. "IP67 WATER RESISTANT" in img_main.png shows smeared letters (the shipped ad_02_main.jpg is clean, so make sure the clean one is what runs).
  • Headline-only mismatch: image v1 says "Wear it to wudu. Keep your count." but the recommended hook (H1) is the main image. Two different on-image messages for the same ad, which will split performance data.

5. Clarity, rhythm, flab, clichés, overlap

  • Primary text. Line 1 is a stack of three fragments with three different ideas (ritual, tools, wet hands). The second paragraph is the false claim. The third and fourth carry the product. The fifth is the off-angle office line. The sixth is the offer. It drifts and the "wudu" thread is dropped after paragraph 3.
  • Rhythm. The short, parallel fragments are good ("Tap to count. Feel a gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99."). "None made for wet hands." is the best line in the ad and should survive in some form.
  • Flab. "The Al Rayan Dhikr Ring 2.0 has IP67 water resistance." is a spec sentence in the middle of a pain story. "Tap Shop Now and wear it to wudu." is cute but the third use of the phrase.
  • Clichés. "needs this" is straight from the swipe (and every lip-balm ad). Acceptable on-image as the swipe structure, but not as the only idea.
  • Overlap with the other 10 ads.
  • "Wear it to wudu / IP67 so wear it to wudu" also appears in ad_03 (bullet) and ad_10 (line). ad_02 should own it; the others can keep a plain IP67 line (note for orchestrator).
  • "Silent tracking, discreet tool at work" overlaps ad_06, ad_09, ad_11 (office). Remove from ad_02.
  • Lost-count / interruption framing is owned by ad_10, ad_05, ad_07 and ad_01. ad_02 must not pivot into it, which is exactly what the "set a counter down" paragraph does.
  • "Your dhikr was never weak / blame the tool" is used in ad_01, ad_09, ad_10. ad_02 uses a mild version via H4; drop it.
  • Hook shape "X. Y. Your ___ needs this." is unique in the set. Keep the structure only if the hook is rewritten to be situation-based.

6. Prioritised list

MUST FIX

  1. Remove the identity assertion from the hook and on-image headline: "Wudu five times a day. Your count needs this." Rewrite so it names a situation or the product, not the viewer's practice (for example five prayers / the sink / wet hands).
  2. Remove or replace the untrue pain: "Set a counter down at the sink and you lose your count.", "None made for wudu." and the whole of H4. Replace with a friction that is true: hands wet and busy, the tool must be put down and picked up, the count lives in your head or in a device you cannot touch with wet hands, a ring on the finger keeps the number in sight and the 33/66/99 pulse tells you where you are.
  3. Fiqh-safe wudu wording everywhere (hook, headline, primary text, CTA line): "stays on through the splashes" / "built for wet hands", never "wear it to wudu" as an absolute and never a picture or line suggesting wudu is done with the ring untouched. One short, plain line that does not preach is enough (the store FAQ's register).
  4. Replace the VOC-22 lift ("A discreet tool you can have all the time at work.") and delete the office drift; it belongs to other ads.
  5. Image fix (clip/re-render): display digits on the right ring are garbled/vertical and the left ring display is unreadable; the ad is about the count, so the count must be legible. Confirm the finger the ring is on in the left panel (man's hand, must not be index/middle). Either re-render or say explicitly that on-image changes are not worth it and keep the current clean ad_02_main.jpg; if kept, the copy must not depend on the digits.

SHOULD FIX

  1. Make the split an actual contrast (left: the wet-hands problem, for example beads or a clicker set aside on the sink edge or a phone screen-down, hands wet and empty-handed; right: the ring) or drop the split.
  2. Cut the "wear it to wudu" repetition to one placement; one headline, one different CTA line.
  3. Replace "Wudu five times a day" with a safe fact ("Five prayers a day. Five trips to the sink." or similar) in whatever line survives.
  4. Replace the dull Meta headline 2 ("Dhikr ring with IP67 water rating") and de-duplicate the offer lines (badges + primary + headline 3 + description say the same two facts).
  5. Fix the beliefs header (B5 then B4 as written, or reorder) and let the copy install the order it claims.
  6. Swap badge 2 "TAP TO COUNT" for something more ownable and true ("33 · 66 · 99 pulses", "Silent tracking") if a re-render happens.
  7. Tell the orchestrator: ad_03 and ad_10 should drop the "wear it to wudu" line so ad_02 owns it.

KEEP (what works)

  • The ritual-callout structure and the cream/green/black layout from the swipe; the clean typography and bold hierarchy of ad_02_main.jpg.
  • "None made for wet hands." (the best sentence; the right tone for the hook if reworked).
  • The IP67 discipline: "water resistant", no waterproof, no shower/swim, no submerged hands.
  • 33/66/99 pulse line, offer line ($49.99, free US shipping, 60-day money-back), "Shop Now".
  • No testimonials, no counts, no metrics, no faith overreach, no ring-earns-reward language.
  • Short sentences and the plain, warm vocabulary (wudu, dhikr).

7. Directions for the rewrite (3-5)

  1. Own the moment, not the spec: "the sink is where the count is most at risk of getting put down." Hands busy and wet, phone/clicker/beads have to be set aside, tasbih stops mid-number. The ring stays on the finger and keeps the number. Hook candidates (no identity assertion): "Hands wet. Count still there." / "The count stays on your finger. Even at the sink." / "Five prayers a day. Five trips to the sink." Primary text lands on the true sentence: IP67 water resistant, built for wet hands and wudu splashes.
  2. A situation-first scene in second person, not "you do wudu". "Sleeves up. Tap running. Where were you at, 61 or 71?" resolves into "the ring still shows it." It is a relatable scenario (CONTEXT option b), not a testimonial, and it drops the "counter set down" falsehood by moving the pain to the dhikr in progress.
  3. Fiqh-literate micro-line, once. "IP67 water resistant: splashes at the sink are fine." Pair it with a line that does not claim wudu is complete in place (optionally "slide it aside when you wash, as you normally would"). This pre-empts the comment-section pedant and builds trust with exactly the audience that reads fiqh.
  4. Give the pulse a job at the sink. After the wet-hands scene, "a gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99 tells you where you are without looking" does the B4 work and ties the wudu angle to the product mechanism, instead of a feature list. Keep it within what the brief allows (do not imply the 100th, do not add reward).
  5. Differentiate from ad_03/05/09/10. Don't use "blame your tools", "lost count" as the headline, or "quiet office". Own: the sink, wet hands, "before salah", "Five prayers", with one CTA that is not the headline repeated ("Tap Shop Now. 60 days to try it."). If the image is re-rendered: left = empty wet hands with a clicker or beads set aside on the sink edge, right = the ring with a legible count, the ring finger (not index/middle) on the left hand, or a woman's hand to avoid the men's-finger question altogether.

After each prayer: SubhanAllah 33, Alhamdulillah 33, Allahu Akbar 34 (Sahih Muslim 596a). Easy to lose count when it is all new.

Black or rose gold. IP67 water resistant. 1-year tech warranty. Free US shipping. $49.99.

Tap Shop Now to choose their size and color.

Editor notes

Verification

  • Faith: no verse or hadith is quoted. It is cited as practice, not attributed to a person's words, and it carries no reward claim. No claim about what the ring earns, no iman/khushu', ring never placed above fingers. The pulse at 33, 66 and 99 is described only as a place-keeper and never as marking the 100th.
  • Wudu: no wudu line anywhere (ad_02 owns it). IP67 appears only as "IP67 water resistant".

Critique of the previous version

ad_03 critique (copy chief pass) - gift for someone new to the deen (A2, ANG5)

Reviewed: 03-ads/ad_03/copy_final.md, brief, chief notes, _assign.json, concept slate, swipe (Hope Rings "If your granddaughter is growing up too fast..."), the images report_media/ad_03_main.jpg, ad_03_img_v3.jpg, 04-assets/ad_03/images/img_v1.png, img_v2.png, all other 10 ads' copy_final.md, the rebuilt store pages, and the full research set.

Overall: legally and faith-wise this is one of the cleaner ads in the run (no testimonial, no metrics, no hadith misuse). The problem is not compliance, it is that the copy is a spec sheet wearing a gift hat. The one thing that makes this ad different from the other ten (the gift buyer, the new practitioner, the emotion of supporting someone) is stated once in line 1 and then abandoned for the same feature bullets every other ad uses. It also ignores the gift buyer's #1 fear (wrong size) and the creative shows a display that cannot be read.

1. Hook strength

A2 is Stage-2 problem-aware (offer_brief): knows the occasion, not the category. The hook has to (a) name the person they are shopping for and (b) make them feel the gift problem. The swipe works because "growing up too fast..." is an emotional trigger and the engraved "Love, Grandma" is an emotional payoff. Ours copies the grammar (callout + ellipsis) but not the emotion: "just started praying" is informational, and the payoff ("5332") is a number, not a feeling, and is not even legible (see section 4).

Ranking of the existing hooks:

Rank Hook Verdict
1 H2 "What do you give a friend who just started praying?" Best fit for A2. It is the actual question in the buyer's head (gift-search intent), names the person and the problem in one line. 10 words is long for an image, but ideal as primary-text line 1.
2 H3 "A card gets forgotten. A ring gets worn." Best rhythm and the only line with a real contrast. Weaknesses: (a) nothing Islamic or dhikr-specific, it could sell any ring to anyone; (b) "A ring gets worn" is an unprovable promise about the recipient; (c) the "card" enemy is a strawman nobody is considering. Works as an image headline only if paired with a line that carries the dhikr context.
3 H1 "If your friend just started praying..." (the on-image headline) Clear callout, strong thumb-stop contrast, mirrors the swipe. But it is a half-sentence that waits for the primary text to finish it, has no emotional charge, and "started praying" is ambiguous (a convert? someone returning to salah?). The second reading carries the shame risk the avatar sheet warns about for A2 ("a gift that implies the receiver is not practising enough"). Acceptable, not strong.
4 H4 "One easy habit they can wear every day." Pure benefit statement, no identity flag, no tension. Generic.
5 H5 "If your husband is new to Islam..." Do not run. Asserts a religious attribute about a person in the viewer's life, which CONTEXT.md names as borderline; it also pushes a male recipient toward a rose-gold ring image and a landing page that tells people to wear it on the index finger (section 3).

Also: primary text line 1 repeats the on-image headline ("If your friend just started praying, give them one easy habit they can wear."). The image and the first line of text are read together in feed, so the second line is wasted. Image = hook, primary line 1 should deepen or answer it, not restate it.

2. Angle, avatar fit, awareness and beliefs

  • Angle (a gift that is practical, not kitsch) fits A2 and is the strongest differentiator in the 11-ad set. Keep it.
  • Awareness: brief says solution-aware, offer_brief says problem-aware. The copy names the category in line 2 ("smart tasbih ring"), which is right. But "The Al Rayan Dhikr Ring 2.0 is a smart tasbih ring." is a flat definition sentence, it does not earn the gift.
  • The ring's best-fit feature for this recipient is buried. Someone who has just started praying is learning the five daily prayers and the 33-33-34 after salah. "Prayer-time alerts for all five daily prayers" and "gentle vibrations at 33, 66 and 99" are the two features that meet that person where they are, and they sit as bullets 2 and 3 next to IP67 with no scene. Nothing in the copy says why a beginner would want this.
  • Belief order B4 -> B5 -> B6 is roughly there, but:
  • B4 is installed as spec ("Tap to count", "silent tracking"), not as a scene.
  • B5 is the thinnest: "A meaningful gift, made for daily use." is an assertion. The only support (IP67, 1-year warranty) is split between a bullet and the last line. The limiting belief "a device is not a meaningful gift" (VOC-25, 35) is asserted away, not argued.
  • B3 is missing entirely. necessary_beliefs.md's gift outline says "permission and respect (B3 in one line)". A Muslim giving a counting device to a new Muslim will hesitate on "is it ok / does it replace the fingers". One line ("Counting on the fingers stays the Sunnah; the ring helps when the count gets long") removes the hesitation, and costs 12 words.
  • The gift buyer's biggest objection is never touched: size. The avatar sheet lists "wrong ring size" as A2's fear (VOC-26, 34). Gift buyers are guessing a size. Allowed: sizes 16-22 mm and the 60-day money-back guarantee. Free size exchange is [CONFIRM] only.
  • "one easy habit" is never made real. A ring does not give a habit. What it can truthfully offer is: count stays on the ring, a pulse at 33/66/99, prayer alerts, and (app) history to see the days add up ([CONFIRM] the app).
  • Emotional layer: the chief itself flagged "fear-of-forgotten-gift beat missing". Nobody feels anything in this copy. A2 buys on emotion/occasion (offer_brief). The feeling available, without guilt: supporting someone quietly, not being preachy, giving something they will actually use daily rather than put on a shelf.

3. Rule violations and risks (quoted)

# Line Rule Severity
1 Bullet: "IP67 water resistance: wear it to wudu" Wudu fiqh. "Wear it to wudu" reads as "leave it on, wudu is done", which CONTEXT.md forbids implying. The comment section for a gift-to-a-new-Muslim ad will include people who know the ruling (move/adjust a ring so water reaches the skin). Safe form: "IP67 water resistant, so wudu splashes are fine." Also a direct overlap with ad_02 whose entire concept is wudu. MUST FIX
2 H5 "If your husband is new to Islam..." (not on image, but in the hook set) Meta personal attributes (religion), also male recipient + rose-gold image + landing page index-finger advice. MUST FIX (cut)
3 Headline option 2 "A Meaningful Gift for a New Muslim" Meta personal attributes: states the recipient's religion in the headline, which is shown to the viewer as "you know someone Muslim". Less severe than H5 because it is third-person, but CONTEXT.md says to prefer situation/product framing. Also "meaningful" is the weakest word in the set. SHOULD FIX
4 H1 / on-image "If your friend just started praying..." Borderline: second-person + religious practice (even of a third party). Lower risk than H5 and acceptable if it stays, but a product-framed line is safer (e.g. "A tasbih they will wear, not lose in a drawer"). SHOULD FIX
5 Headline option 3 "Gift One Easy Daily Dhikr Habit" "Gift ... Dhikr" can read as gifting the dhikr or its reward (the dropped Sadaqah Jariyah idea in disguise). Not a violation, but it sits close to the rule "say what the dhikr earns, never what the ring earns". Cut. SHOULD FIX
6 "It looks like a ring, not a gadget." Allowed (A-10) in words, but the image shows a wide flat band with a big black LCD panel, which reads as a smart band. The claim is contradicted by the creative (section 4). It is also used in ad_05, ad_06 and ad_11. SHOULD FIX
7 "A meaningful gift, made for daily use." "made for daily use" is an implied durability claim with no basis other than IP67/warranty; "meaningful" is empty. SHOULD FIX
8 Image: green gift box (all variants) The copy says nothing about packaging and the brief admits "we have no box photo". A viewer will assume the ring arrives in a presentation box. The user doc's "turnkey luxury packaging" is a recommendation, not a stated Al Rayan fact. If copy ever leans on "gift-ready" or "arrives boxed", it needs [CONFIRM: ships in gift box]. SHOULD FIX (flag to owner)
9 Any size statement Landing page rebuild says 3 sizes (18/20/22 mm) and "free size exchanges"; research/product page says 16-22 mm, 6 sizes, exchange UNCONFIRMED. Do not state a count of sizes in the ad; say "sizes 16-22 mm" only per CONTEXT.md and tag exchange [CONFIRM: free size exchanges]. NOTE for owner

Not violated (checked): no hadith/Quran used (none required); no testimonial or first-person customer voice; no store metrics, stars or counts; no superlatives; no competitor names; no sale price; no IP68/"waterproof"; no riya' bragging; no finger instruction; "rose gold" never implied to be gold; "silent tracking" is brand wording. Prayer alerts are allowed ("five daily prayers"). Note the rebuilt product page says alerts are set in the app, so a recipient needs the app for them; if the copy leans on alerts, consider [CONFIRM: app]. Landing-page warning (outside this ad's scope but it receives this traffic): the rebuilt product/index pages still carry fake testimonials, "8,200+ wearers", "4.9", a "Sarah W., New York" quote and an index-finger recommendation. A gift ad for husbands/brothers that lands there is exposed.

4. Copy-to-creative mismatch

What the images show (all four variants are the same concept: rose-gold ring standing in an open box on satin, no hands, no people):

  • The display is not legible in any variant. ad_03_main.jpg shows garbled seven-segment glyphs (reads roughly "2655"), img_v1/img_v2 show the digits stacked vertically down the band, and img_v3 is the only one with a horizontal near-"5332" reading. The run log confirms: "ad_02/ad_03 a2 FAIL (vertical digits) -> kept best_attempt 2, flagged". The copy says "A small display shows the number" and the spec lists DISPLAY_TEXT "5332" as the reveal. The reveal fails in the creative that was chosen as the main image. A gift buyer scrutinising a $49.99 product will notice nonsense digits. A re-render is already justified, so any on-image text change costs nothing extra.
  • "It looks like a ring, not a gadget": the ring in frame is a wide flat band dominated by a black LCD plus a logo plate. It looks like a smart band. Soften or drop.
  • Box: not mentioned in copy, but implied (item 8 above).
  • Rose gold: fine for "friend" generically; a poor match for any husband/brother/father variant (use black there; never show a man wearing it on the index finger).
  • On-image text quality: main uses a condensed heavy grotesque in black on cream with the line break "If your friend just / started praying..." (leaves "just" hanging on line 1). v2/v3 break better ("If your friend / just started praying..."). v3 (cream on deep green) has the best contrast and the most premium feel. The spec says heavy grotesque, but the four variants use four different type styles, so any variant test is confounded by font.
  • The headline is a dangling ellipsis whose resolution lives only in the primary text. With the image alone, the viewer sees "If your friend just started praying..." + ring in a box and must infer "gift". That is OK for this audience but makes the on-image line weaker than the swipe's, whose image resolves the emotion.
  • Recommendation: use v3 or a new render with the display fixed; if re-rendering, replace H1 with a line that stands alone.

5. Clarity, rhythm, flab, clichés, repetition, overlap

  • Rhythm: 18 sentences averaging 5.3 words, all declarative. It reads like a product list, with no turn and no breath. The chief praised "FK 1.7"; for a gift ad that is monotone, not clean.
  • Flab / empty: "A meaningful gift, made for daily use." ; "one easy habit" (vague); "Silent tracking" (reader asks: tracking what?); "Tap Shop Now to get them one." (weak, "get them one" has no emotion).
  • Clichés: "meaningful gift", "easy habit", "A ring gets worn" (common gift-copy construction), "not a gadget".
  • Redundancy inside the ad: line 1 repeats the on-image text; headline #1 repeats the price already in the last paragraph; Meta description "60-day money-back guarantee" repeats a line already in the primary text and headline option 3 in other ads.
  • Overlap with the other 10 ads (skimmed all copy_final.md):
  • The mechanism stack ("Tap to count your dhikr. ... Gentle vibrations at 33, 66 and 99. Silent tracking") appears nearly verbatim in ad_01, 02, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08, 09, 10, 11. ad_03 adds nothing of its own.
  • "wear it to wudu" / IP67 bullet duplicates ad_02 (its concept) and ad_10.
  • "It looks like a ring" duplicates ad_05, ad_06, ad_11.
  • "Tap Shop Now and ..." closing duplicates every ad.
  • Unique to ad_03 and worth protecting: the gift buyer, the "just started praying" recipient, the "card forgotten / ring worn" contrast.
  • Meta headline #1 "Smart Tasbih Ring Gift, $49.99": product-first, no pull; it also duplicates what the image already says. Headline #2 and #3 (see section 3).

MUST FIX

  1. Remove "wear it to wudu" from the IP67 bullet; use "IP67 water resistant, so wudu splashes are fine" or drop the bullet, since ad_02 owns wudu.
  2. Cut H5 (husband / new to Islam) and any headline asserting the recipient's religion ("a New Muslim"); prefer situation/product framing or brand voice.
  3. Fix the unreadable display in the creative (re-render) and make the on-image text match what is shown; do not claim the display shows "5332" while the image shows garbage.
  4. Make primary text line 1 do different work from the on-image headline (right now it repeats it).
  5. Address the gift buyer's size fear: "sizes 16-22 mm" + 60-day money-back guarantee, and [CONFIRM: free size exchanges] if exchange is mentioned.
  6. Replace the generic feature stack with a recipient scene (learning the five prayers / the count after salah) so the ad stops being a copy of the other ten.

SHOULD FIX

  1. Drop or soften "It looks like a ring, not a gadget" (contradicted by the image, repeated in 3 other ads).
  2. Add one B3 line honouring the Sunnah of finger counting.
  3. Replace "A meaningful gift, made for daily use." with something concrete about what the recipient does with it.
  4. Move the warranty next to the durability claim so B5 is argued, not asserted.
  5. Rewrite the Meta headlines (product-first headline duplicates the image) and use the description for something other than a repeat of the guarantee.
  6. Resolve the box implication: keep it unbranded and say nothing, or tag [CONFIRM: ships in a gift box] if packaging is mentioned.
  7. Decide whether the hook is about a convert or someone returning to salah; avoid the shame reading.
  8. Keep fonts consistent between image variants so tests are clean.

KEEP (what works)

  • The gift-buyer angle and the "friend who just started praying" recipient: the most distinct concept in the run.
  • Callout-with-ellipsis structure and the strong black-on-cream / cream-on-green contrast (v3 is the best-looking creative).
  • H3 "A card gets forgotten. A ring gets worn." as a rhythm model.
  • H2 as the sharpest buyer-voice question.
  • Allowed-claims discipline (no metrics, no hadith misuse, no unconfirmed claims), "silent tracking" wording, the offer line ($49.99, free US shipping, 60-day money-back, 1-year warranty), and a single CTA.
  • The primary-text instinct to name the category in line 2 for a problem-aware buyer.

3-5 directions for the rewrite

  1. Scene over spec. Open the primary text with the buyer's question (H2 form) and answer it with a concrete recipient scene: the person learning the five prayers and the tasbih after salah (33, 33, 34), who does not have to hold the count in their head because the ring pulses at 33, 66 and 99 (do not imply it marks 100) and buzzes at prayer times. Use prayer alerts as the beginner-specific benefit.
  2. Answer the buyer's fears in two short lines. One for "will it be used / is it cheap" (IP67, 1-year warranty, 60-day money-back), one for "wrong size" (sizes 16-22 mm; [CONFIRM: free size exchanges]). Add one respectful line that finger counting stays the Sunnah.
  3. New image headline that stands alone and avoids the religion assertion, e.g. a contrast line in the H3 family but with dhikr context (a tasbih they will wear vs one that ends up in a drawer), bundled with the re-render that fixes the display. Primary line 1 then continues the thought rather than repeating it.
  4. Cut everything shared with the other ads: no wudu bullet, no "looks like a ring", no copy-pasted mechanism sentence. Give ad_03 its own compact proof set (prayer alerts, count stays on the ring, 33/66/99 pulse, warranty) and let the other ads own wudu, silence and phone.
  5. Brand-voice or product framing only. If any warmth is needed beyond the scene, write it as "We made this for..." or as a second-person scenario, never as a customer story, and keep gifts for men on the black ring image with no finger direction.

ad_04 copy — FINAL (ship-ready) — "Four notifications. One was a dhikr reminder."

Primary text paste-ready

Four notifications. One was a dhikr reminder.
The rest: posts, group chat, a cart.
Al Rayan Dhikr Ring 2.0 has none of those.

A tasbih app is a fine tool. It just lives on the same screen as the feed, so a quick count can turn into forty minutes.

A ring is simpler. Tap to count. The number shows on the ring's own small display. A gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99 marks your place.
Nothing to scroll. Nothing to unlock. [CONFIRM: works without your phone]

Counting on the fingers is the Sunnah. The ring is there when the count runs into the hundreds.

$49.99. Free US shipping. 60-day money-back guarantee.
Tap Shop Now.

  1. A phone has a feed. A ring has a count. (39) ← recommended
  2. Dhikr on your hand. Nothing to scroll. (38)
  3. Tap to count. No feed to open. (30)

Meta description · CTA

60-day money-back guarantee

  • Button: Shop Now
  • Line: "$49.99. Free US shipping. 60-day money-back guarantee. Tap Shop Now."
  • Offer: $49.99, free US shipping. No sale price, no % off.
  • Risk reversal: 60-day money-back guarantee (1-year tech warranty is an alternate description).

Word count: primary text 110 words (115 with the CONFIRM tag).
