Editor notes: changes, checks, re-render, owner to confirm

Editor notes

What I changed from the rewrite, and why

  1. Meta headlines reworked. The rewrite's "Dhikr on your finger. Nothing to scroll." (exactly 40 characters, no margin) put "dhikr" and "finger" together, which ad_01's editor deliberately avoided because it muddles with Sunnah finger counting. New headline 1 is the screen-versus-ring contrast (39 chars), which matches the picture (phone screen vs the ring's lit display) and reads as a visual, not a claim. Headline 2 swaps "finger" for "hand" (38). Headline 3 kept (30). Dropped "No feed on your finger." for the same finger reason.
  2. H6 replaced. "We hear this a lot..." asserts customer feedback we cannot verify, and ad_01's editor cut the same device. New H6 is a plain observation. The old H5 became the contrast H4, and the "dhikr on your hand" line is now H5.
  3. Sunnah line reworded. "The ring is for the long counts and busy hands" was near-identical to ad_08 ("a helper for long counts and busy hands") and close to ad_06 and ad_01. New line: "Counting on the fingers is the Sunnah. The ring is there when the count runs into the hundreds." Same respect, no ranking above finger counting, distinct wording.
  4. Mechanism tightened. "tells you where you are without looking down" over-claimed (the pulse only fires at 33, 66, 99, and you look at the display for the number). Now: "The number shows on the ring's own small display. A gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99 marks your place." Accurate, does not imply the 100th, ties to the display visible in the picture.
  5. CONFIRM scoped narrowly. "Nothing to scroll." is true of any ring (no feed) and needs no tag. Only "Nothing to unlock." depends on the unconfirmed works-without-your-phone claim, so the tag now sits on that one sentence alone and can be deleted cleanly.
  6. Phone line tightened. "A tasbih app is a fine tool. It just lives on the same screen as the feed, so a quick count can turn into forty minutes." Cut "opening it for one count" (clunky), hedged ("can turn into"), no app-bashing, no assertion about the viewer.
  7. On-image headline status clarified. The rewrite listed the rendered second-person headline as "kept" with the neutral version optional. For Meta personal-attributes safety at the most visible spot in the feed, the neutral version ("the phone", not "your phone") is now the primary listed version, with the rendered text as fallback. Same eight words, same layout.
  8. Verified: all CRITIQUE MUST FIXES resolved. 1 (personal attributes/shame): no "you do dhikr", no "weak intention" anywhere. 2 (untagged phone claim): tagged. 3 (Quran 2:152): cut, no scripture in this ad, so nothing to misattribute and no reward-for-the-ring reading. 4 (VOC in quote marks): none. 5 (line 1 duplicates headline): line 1 is a new beat and the ring is named inside 125 characters. 6 (overlap): no "friction", "blame your tools", "discreet at work", clicker or beads lines.
  9. Hard-rule scan: no store metrics, stars, sale price, % off, IP68, "waterproof", weight, battery, delivery speed, superlatives, competitor names. "Silent tracking" not used (not needed here). No wudu, riya', men's-finger or rose-gold lines. No testimonial, no first person. No hadith or verse, so no attribution risk.
  10. Overlap check against ad_01 to ad_11 (rewrites/finals present): the notification/feed lane, "Four notifications", "A phone has a feed", "Nothing to scroll/unlock", "forty minutes" appear in no other ad. ad_02 mentions the phone only as an object at the sink; ad_08 uses "eyes on the street", not the feed. The Sunnah line is now unique.

Re-render needed

  • Recommended (small): on-image HEADLINE Opened your phone for dhikr. to Opened the phone for dhikr. 40 minutes ago. (second-person plus religious practice is borderline under Meta personal-attributes policy). Same layout.
  • Optional, batch with the above: enlarge the headline or lift it above the phone (about 5% cap height now); put the ring on the ring finger or pinky rather than the thumb (hand reads male; thumb is not index/middle so it is not a rule breach, but it shows the tap gesture as awkward). Do not move it to the index or middle finger.
  • If img_v3 (train) is the version that runs, apply the same headline change.
  • If no re-render: ad is acceptable but carries residual personal-attributes risk on the image text only.

Owner to confirm

  • [CONFIRM: works without your phone] — one sentence ("Nothing to unlock."). Rebuilt store says the ring counts on its own and the app is optional; not confirmed by the owner. Delete the sentence if not confirmed.
  • Decide on the on-image headline re-render and ring-finger placement (above).
  • Landing page check before traffic: rebuilt product and home pages say "Most people wear it on the index finger" (men's ring rule) and carry unconfirmed claims and testimonial/metric content flagged in other ads' notes; fix on the store, not in this ad.
Critique of the previous version

ad_04 copy critique (COPY CRITIC) — "Opened your phone for dhikr. 40 minutes ago."

Ad: image 9:16 · native-notifications · ANG4 phone-free remembrance · A1 · problem-aware, sophistication 3-4 · beliefs B2 then B4. Reviewed: 03-ads/ad_04/copy_final.md, brief, chief notes v1, creative report_media/ad_04_main.jpg (+ img_v1, img_v3), the other 10 ads' copy, and the rebuilt store pages.

Verdict in one paragraph

The concept and the headline are the strongest thing in this ad: a lock screen that reads as the viewer's own phone, one specific scene, and a payoff line ("40 minutes ago") that lands in a beat. The primary text does not live up to it. Its first line re-states the headline (so the most valuable visible text in the feed is wasted), it says the Muslim viewer "get[s] pulled into social media" and has "weak intention" (second-person plus religious practice plus negative self-perception, which is Meta personal-attributes territory and also violates our no-shame rule), it implies the ring works without the phone with no [CONFIRM] tag, it ends on a Quran verse that is stuck between the feature list and the price with no attribution lead-in, and half of it is boilerplate shared with 9 other ads (including a near-verbatim line from ad_02 and the whole "friction / never your intention" reframe from ad_01 and ad_05). The ad has one job nobody else in the set has: the phone-and-feed trap. The rewrite should own that territory exclusively and drop the "blame your tools" and "discreet at work" lines.

1. Hook strength

What the feed shows: image first (lock screen, headline inside the phone, lamp-lit couch, ring on the hand), then primary text line 1 above it, then the Meta headline under it. In Meta's feed only about the first 125 characters of primary text show before "See more", so what the A1 sees is: image + You picked up your phone for dhikr. Then the feed picked you. You get pulled into social media. That is not weak intention. The product is not named until line 3, which is behind "See more".

Ranking of the six existing hooks (for A1 scroll-stop; A2 gift buyers are not the target and would correctly skip this):

Rank Hook Why
1 H1 "Opened your phone for dhikr. 40 minutes ago." Specific scene, fragment rhythm, the second sentence is the punchline, instant self-recognition (VOC-30 felt, not quoted). Weaknesses: "40 minutes ago" is slightly ambiguous on first read (opened it 40 minutes ago and still on it, yes, but it takes half a beat); "Opened your phone for dhikr" assigns a religious practice to the viewer (see section 3). Still the best of the six.
2 H4 "It's not your intention. It's the phone." Clean pattern interrupt, installs B2 in seven words. But it presupposes the viewer doubted their intention (shame-adjacent) and it blames "the phone" in general, which the research says to avoid (do not label apps invalid; emphasise freedom from screen distraction). Better as a mid-copy turn than as the opener.
3 H6 "Why does dhikr always end in the feed?" Neutral voice, no "you", curiosity. "always" over-claims and "end in the feed" is clunky. Good as an alternate that avoids the personal-attributes risk.
4 H5 "Dhikr on your finger. Phone in your pocket." Best rhythm in the set and a good Meta headline, but it is a solution line, not a scroll-stopper for a problem-aware reader. "Phone in your pocket" implies the ring works without the phone ([CONFIRM] claim).
5 H3 "Opened your phone for dhikr? Stop scrolling." "Stop scrolling" is the most worn-out hook in paid social. Throws away the 40-minute punchline. (It is the headline of img_v1, which is the weaker render for that reason.)
6 H2 "I get pulled into social media." Sound familiar? Puts VOC-30 in quote marks, which reads as a customer quote (banned), and "Sound familiar?" is a cliché.

Primary-text line 1 as a hook: You picked up your phone for dhikr. Then the feed picked you. Rhythm is good ("Then the feed picked you" is the best sentence in the whole copy block), but it says the same thing as the image headline. Image says it, line 1 says it again, line 2 says it a third time ("You get pulled into social media"). Three beats for one idea before the viewer learns what the ad is for.

2. Angle, avatar fit, awareness, belief order

  • Angle and avatar fit well. A1 is exactly the person who has felt VOC-30 (phone apps pull them into social media), and the research lists "phone-free remembrance" as ANG4 with strong evidence. Problem-aware matches: no need to explain what a smart tasbih ring is, but a stage 3-4 market also needs the mechanism, which is where the copy is thin (below).
  • Belief order B2 then B4 is respected structurally, but neither belief is actually installed well:
  • B2 ("what stops me is friction, not my intention") is asserted, not shown. That is not weak intention. It is friction, and friction has a fix. is a copywriter's rhythm trick ("X has a fix") with an analyst's word ("friction"). A1 does not say "friction". The ad has a perfect visual for B2 (the reminder is one notification in a pile of three others) and never uses it in words.
  • B4 ("a ring I wear can count silently, without a phone, noise or a look at a screen") is delivered as a feature list: Tap to count your dhikr. A gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99. The link to the phone trap is missing. The pulse matters here because it tells you where you are without lighting a screen; the display matters because the count lives on the ring. Neither connection is made. Nothing says there is no feed, no inbox and nothing to unlock on a ring.
  • Silent tracking, so the phone stays in your pocket. is a non sequitur. Silence is the clicker/office benefit (ad_09, ad_05, ad_06). The phone-trap benefit is "nothing to open", not "silent".
  • Missing objection handling that a fiqh-aware comment section will raise: nothing about fingers/Sunnah. For this angle it is not mandatory, but one clause would pre-empt "so you replace the Sunnah with a gadget?" Ads 01 and 07 carry it; see SHOULD FIX.
  • Sophistication: the copy leans on product features that every competitor also lists (OLED, vibration, app sync). The differentiated mechanism in this ad is the absence of a feed and notifications on the finger. Right now that is unsaid.

3. Rule violations (quote the line)

# Rule Offending line Problem
V1 Meta personal attributes (religion / religious practice implied about the viewer) HEADLINE and line 1: Opened your phone for dhikr. / You picked up your phone for dhikr. Second-person plus religious practice. CONTEXT.md calls this borderline and says to prefer situation/product framing or brand voice. It is the first thing a viewer reads in the feed (line 1).
V2 Meta personal attributes + no "you" + negative self-perception; no guilt or shame Line 2: You get pulled into social media. That is not weak intention. Asserts a behaviour about "you" and introduces the idea that the viewer's intention might be weak, to then relieve it. The chief notes literally describe this as "shame of 'my intention is weak' relieved". We do not install shame in order to remove it. Also H4 It's not your intention. It's the phone. has the same presupposition.
V3 Never put VOC in quote marks as if a customer said it H2 "I get pulled into social media." Sound familiar? Quote marks turn paraphrase-risk VOC-30 into an apparent testimonial. Line 2 also lifts the VOC-30 wording nearly verbatim without marks, which is acceptable as avatar language but should be reshaped into our own voice.
V4 [CONFIRM] claim used untagged: works without your phone Line 4 Silent tracking, so the phone stays in your pocket. and H5 Phone in your pocket. "Phone stays in your pocket" tells the reader the ring counts and displays on its own. The store page says so ("Do I need my phone with me? No."), but the owner has not confirmed it, so it needs [CONFIRM: works without your phone] or a wording that only claims what is confirmed (the ring has its own small display showing the count).
V5 Faith: attribution, and never tie reward to the ring; say what the dhikr earns Line 5 "So remember Me; I will remember you. And be grateful to Me and do not deny Me" (Quran 2:152) Wording is exact to offer_brief row 2 (good). Problems: (a) no "Allah says" lead-in, so "Me" and "I" have no speaker for a reader skimming; (b) it sits directly after A discreet tool you can wear all day, even at work. and directly before the price, so "I will remember you" reads as the reward for buying and wearing the ring, which is the exact reading the rules forbid; (c) a non sequitur with the phone-trap story. Scripture as a pre-CTA garnish is also an adab risk for a comment section that will notice.
V6 Duplicate / templated line from another ad Line 4 A discreet tool you can wear all day, even at work. Near-verbatim of ad_02 line A discreet tool you can have all the time at work. (both lifted from VOC-22). Also "even at work" drags in ANG1 (ads 06 and 11), and the picture is a couch at night, not work.
V7 Claim hygiene check (clean) n/a No store metrics, stars, review counts, sale price, % off, IP68, "waterproof", delivery speed, weight, battery, superlatives, competitor names. "Silent tracking" is brand wording. "Gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99" is fine and does not imply the 100th. No wudu, riya', men's-finger or rose-gold copy issue in the text. No fabricated testimonial. $49.99, free US shipping and the 60-day money-back guarantee match the store. Keep all of this.
V8 Men's finger (visual flag, not copy) n/a See section 4: the ring in all three renders sits on the thumb of a hand that reads male. That is not the index or middle finger, so it does not break the rule, but it is not how the product is worn either. No visual shows index-finger wear.

Other items checked and clean: no testimonials, "I" voice, riya' bragging (the ring display shows 5332 as it does on every product shot; copy does not mention counts), no "completely silent", no claim the ring raises iman or earns reward in the text itself (apart from the proximity problem in V5), no claim above Sunnah finger counting.

4. Copy-to-creative mismatch

What the main image actually shows: a hand (looks male: large, hairy) holding a phone upright, couch and warm lamp at night. The lock screen carries the headline Opened your phone for dhikr. 40 minutes ago. in large white type, then four stacked cards: Time for dhikr, New posts today, New group messages, Cart is waiting. The black ring with the gold logo button and display 5332 is on the thumb of the same hand, at lower right. All strings are spelled correctly and the render looks native.

Mismatches and issues:

  1. The picture shows the ring on the hand that is holding the phone. The copy says the ring is why the phone stays in your pocket. The image is the "before" and the "after" in one frame, and nothing on the image says what the ring is or does. A scroller sees a hand, a phone and a small black ring that could be a smartwatch band. Only the primary text, behind "See more", names the product. Copy must carry that bridge in the first 125 characters, or the ring has no meaning in this ad.
  2. The ring is on the thumb in all three renders (main, v1, v3), although the prompt asked for the ring finger. Store copy says to press the side button with your thumb. A thumb ring means the "tap to count" gesture is shown as impossible. Not a fiqh violation (thumb, not index or middle) and not worth a re-render by itself, but copy must not describe where or how the ring is worn in a way the image contradicts. If a re-render ever happens for another reason, put it on the ring finger.
  3. Scene is a couch at night (main) or a train (v3). Line 4 says "even at work". Neither scene is work. Delete the work line (it also overlaps ads 06 and 11).
  4. Time logic: headline says you opened the phone 40 minutes ago; the screen shows a locked screen with a pile of notifications. A viewer will not stumble on this (the pile reads as what pulled you away) but the copy can use it: the reminder is just one of four things on that screen.
  5. On-image text quality: spelling and layout are clean, there is no CTA-looking button, no badges, no real logos. The headline is on the phone screen at roughly 5% cap height, about a third of the frame width; at 9:16 feed size it reads as lock-screen text (good for native feel) but is smaller than it should be for a scroll-stop. Not a copy fix; if a re-render is budgeted, enlarge the headline or lift it above the phone. No re-render is needed for the copy rewrite.
  6. Time for dhikr implies the viewer's tasbih app is installed and has reminders. Fine for a persona-neutral spoof, but do not write copy that bashes tasbih apps (the user's research says do not label apps invalid).
  7. Cart is waiting is the one card that is not "social". It is a nice touch (a shopping nudge is part of the pile) but makes the scene feel retargeted; harmless.
  8. img_v1 (headline Opened your phone for dhikr? Stop scrolling.) and img_v3 (train, same headline as main) share the copy. Whatever headline the rewrite picks must either match the render being run or be flagged as an on-image change.
  9. Meta headline Keep your dhikr off the screen clashes with the creative: the ring has its own screen (the lit digits are the focal point), and the headline reads as an anti-screen slogan.

5. Clarity, rhythm, flab, clichés, repetition, overlap

Line-by-line:

  • L1 You picked up your phone for dhikr. Then the feed picked you. Good rhythm; best sentence in the block. Repeats the headline. Cut or move.
  • L2 You get pulled into social media. That is not weak intention. It is friction, and friction has a fix. Three sentences, one idea, a jargon word ("friction") and a stock formula ("X has a fix"). The VOC lift is also the weakest, most generic phrasing of the pain.
  • L3 Al Rayan Dhikr Ring 2.0 keeps your count on your finger. Tap to count your dhikr. A gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99. "count" three times in 20 words. First product mention arrives in the 3rd paragraph. The pulse has no reason attached.
  • L4 Silent tracking, so the phone stays in your pocket. A discreet tool you can wear all day, even at work. Non sequitur ("silent" explains pocket), V4, V6.
  • L5 Quran 2:152: V5.
  • L6 Tap Shop Now. $49.99, free US shipping, 60-day money-back guarantee. Same close appears in ads 01, 05, 06, 07, 11 and more. Fine, but also repeated verbatim in headline option 3.
  • Meta headline 1 Keep your dhikr off the screen: vague, negative, contradicts the ring's display (section 4.9). Headline 2 Tap to count. Pulse at 33, 66, 99. is a stock feature line that appears in ad_10 and ad_11 too. Headline 3 is pure offer; fine as a third option.
  • Meta description Free US shipping: weak but harmless; could carry the 60-day guarantee instead of repeating a body line.

Overlap with the other 10 ads (this is the bigger problem):

This ad's line Also in
It is friction / not weak intention ad_01 "So it was never my intention. It was the friction: the phone, the beads, the lost count." ad_05 "That is friction in the tool, not weakness in you." ad_09/10 "Your dhikr was never the problem/weak. Your tools were."
Phone-trap story (reached for phone, ended in the feed) ad_01 body ("I reached for my phone to open the counter. Next thing I knew I was deep in my feed") and ad_08 H3 ("You open your dhikr app and get pulled into social media")
Tap to count your dhikr. A gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99. ads 01, 02, 05, 06, 07, 08, 10, 11 (almost identical sentence)
Silent tracking + A discreet tool you can ... at work ad_02 (identical to the second sentence), ad_05, ad_06, ad_09, ad_11
Tap Shop Now. $49.99, free US shipping, 60-day money-back guarantee. nearly every ad

Ad_04 is the only ad whose assigned angle is the phone trap, yet its own body text spends its middle on the "blame the tools" frame owned by ads 01, 05, 09 and 10, and on the "silent at work" frame owned by ads 02, 06 and 11. That dilutes the one thing that makes it different.

Cliché inventory: "Stop scrolling" (H3), "Sound familiar?" (H2), "friction has a fix", "Shop Now" doubled with the button.

MUST FIX

  1. V1/V2 personal attributes and shame. Rewrite line 1, line 2 and the hook so they describe the situation or speak in brand voice rather than asserting "you" do dhikr, "you" get pulled into social media and might have "weak intention". Keep the 40-minute scene.
  2. V4 untagged phone claim. Either tag [CONFIRM: works without your phone] on any "phone stays in your pocket / phone in your pocket / nothing to unlock" line, or reword to a confirmed fact (the ring has its own small display showing the count; tap to count).
  3. V5 Quran 2:152 placement and attribution. If a verse stays: exact wording from the verified table, with an "Allah says" lead-in, separated from the product and the price (not between the feature list and the CTA), with no line that lets "I will remember you" read as a reward for wearing the ring. If it cannot be placed cleanly, cut it; this ad does not need scripture to work.
  4. V3 VOC in quote marks. Drop H2 as written. Any hook that reuses VOC-30 must not carry quote marks or attribution.
  5. Primary text line 1 duplicates the on-image headline. The first 125 characters must do new work: ideally add the second beat of the scene and name the ring before "See more" (the viewer currently never learns what the ad is selling above the fold).
  6. V6 overlap. Remove A discreet tool you can wear all day, even at work (ad_02 twin, wrong scene) and the "friction / not your intention / tools" reframe (owned by ads 01, 05, 09, 10).

SHOULD FIX

  1. Replace the unlinked feature list with one mechanism line tied to the trap: what a ring has that a phone screen does not (nothing to unlock, no feed on your finger, the count is on the ring's own display, a pulse at 33/66/99 so you know where you are without looking). Keep it to confirmed facts or tag it.
  2. Say the thing the picture shows and the copy ignores: "Time for dhikr" is one of four notifications. Use the stack in words.
  3. Fix the Meta headline: Keep your dhikr off the screen is vague and the ring has a display. Stronger options use the H5 rhythm or a concrete benefit (examples under directions). Keep to 40 characters.
  4. Add a single clause that keeps fingers on top: counting on the fingers stays the Sunnah, the ring is for the long counts and busy hands. One line, no wobble, as in ads 01 and 07 (do not copy their wording).
  5. Cut the repeated words ("count" x3, "dhikr" x4 in lines 3 and 4) and tighten rhythm to short lines; remove "Tap Shop Now" if the button already says it, or keep it once as the only CTA.
  6. Meta description: use it for the second offer fact (e.g. the 60-day money-back guarantee) instead of repeating shipping.
  7. If any render is redone later (not required for this copy round): headline larger or above the phone, ring on the ring finger, not the thumb.

KEEP (what works)

  • The scene and the headline Opened your phone for dhikr. 40 minutes ago. (the visual, the specificity, the punchline). Only the personal-attribute framing needs softening, not the idea.
  • Then the feed picked you. as a line (best sentence in the copy) in some form.
  • The notification stack as a storytelling device; the "Time for dhikr" card being the top, ignored one.
  • Correct, exact use of confirmed product facts: tap to count, small display, gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99, "silent tracking" wording, $49.99, free US shipping, 60-day money-back guarantee.
  • No store metrics, no testimonials, no sale price, no superlatives: the ad is clean on truth rules.
  • No button-looking elements on the image (Meta rule).
  • Quran 2:152 wording is verified (if a verse is kept, the words are right; placement and attribution are the issue).
  • H4 "It's not your intention. It's the phone." as an idea for a mid-copy turn (reworded), H5 Dhikr on your finger. Phone in your pocket. as a headline rhythm (tag or reword the phone claim).

Directions for the rewrite (pick one lead, keep the rest consistent)

  1. Narrate the screen, own the phone-trap territory (recommended). First lines describe the pile in the image from the brand's side, no "you do dhikr": the reminder is one notification; the other three are the feed, the group chat, the cart. Then the turn: a ring has none of those. Gives line 1 new information beyond the headline and sets up B2 without the word "friction". Example hook seeds (not final copy): "Four notifications. One of them was your reminder." / "The dhikr reminder was the first notification. The feed was the other three."
  2. Brand-voice scene instead of "you". Open with "We hear this a lot:" and a paraphrased situation (opened a tasbih app, thirty or forty minutes later still scrolling) with no quote marks and no testimonial attribution, or with a plain situation statement ("A tasbih app lives on the same screen as the feed."). Avoids V1/V2/V3 together and sidesteps the personal-attributes policy.
  3. Make the mechanism the payoff of the scene. One short block explaining what a ring changes: tap on the ring, the count sits on its own small display, a gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99 tells you where you are, so there is nothing to unlock and nothing to scroll. Tag [CONFIRM: works without your phone] on the standalone idea, or restrict to the confirmed "small display shows the count". Do not use "silent" as the reason; keep "silent tracking" only where noise is the point.
  4. Faith as a quiet anchor, correctly attributed, away from the sale. Choose one verified line that fits "remembrance in dead moments" (Tirmidhi 3375 with narrator attribution, or Quran 33:41, or keep 2:152 with "Allah says"), place it before the product mention or after a clear paragraph break, say what the dhikr earns not the ring, and keep the CTA block clean and separate. Optionally add the one-line Sunnah note (fingers remain the Sunnah; the ring helps with the long count).
  5. Headline and CTA that match the picture. Examples to test (each under 40 characters): "Dhikr on your finger. Nothing to scroll." / "Tap to count. No feed to open." / "No feed on your finger." Offer block stays $49.99, free US shipping, 60-day money-back guarantee, stated once in primary text with Shop Now as the button only; use the Meta description for the guarantee or the 1-year tech warranty.

Overlap guardrails for the rewrite: do not use "blame your tools / never your intention", do not use "discreet at work", clickers and beads, or the "Tap to count your dhikr. A gentle pulse at 33, 66 and 99." boilerplate verbatim. This ad's lane is: the phone, the feed, the notification pile, and a ring that has none of it.

Editor notes

What changed from the rewrite, and why

  1. On-image headline "Which one comes to the meeting?" → "Which one fits a quiet room?" Meeting/lecture framing was flagged elsewhere in this round as a divided-attention adab issue (ad_06, ad_11) and "meeting" is the scene ad_06 and ad_09 H3 own. "Quiet room" keeps the question, answers the clicker contrast, and assumes no workplace. The image is being re-rendered anyway, so no extra cost.
  2. Primary line 1 rewritten. The rewrite opened "A quiet room. A clicker would turn every head." That is the same scene as ad_09 H3 ("The meeting goes quiet. The clicker doesn't.") and makes the clicker the villain again. New line 1 is the honest role split ("Beads suit the prayer mat. A clicker suits an empty room. A smart tasbih ring suits the rest of your day."): it answers the image question, names the category before "See more", and the three-beat is role-based, not the object list ad_02 uses.
  3. Removed "lecture, waiting room or meeting" (ad_11 owns the waiting room; meeting/lecture carries the adab flag). Scenes are now generic ("quiet room", "rest of your day").
  4. Sunnah guard line reworded from "counting on the fingers stays the Sunnah. The ring is a helper..." (near-identical to ad_01 and ad_10) to "It doesn't replace the beads, and fingers remain the Sunnah. It's one more tool, for the long counts." Keeps the ring below finger counting, no "busy hands" (ad_10).
  5. Cut "lets you keep your eyes up". It overclaims (you still look at the display for the exact count) and echoes ad_08 ("EYES UP / eyes on the street"). Now: the display shows where you are, the vibration at 33, 66 and 99 "marks the way". No implication that the ring marks a 100th.
  6. "Built to be kept" → "Backed by a 1-year tech warranty and a 60-day money-back guarantee." "Built to be kept" is an unprovable durability claim; the warranty and guarantee are the allowed facts and answer the clone-dies fear (Q11). Offer line uses the exact "60-day money-back guarantee" wording.
  7. CTA "Shop Now and try it for 60 days." → "Shop Now." "Try it for 60 days" is now used in ad_09 and ad_11; 60 days already appears once in the offer line.

Old #2 "Built to be kept. 60 days to try." echoed the durability claim and ad_09 H6 / ad_02 headline territory; old #3 "The ring for the quiet room" is now headline 2 as "A smart tasbih ring for quiet rooms" (names the category, 35 chars). New #3 "60 days to try. 1-year warranty." (32).

Critique of the previous version

ad_05 critique (COPY CRITIC) — "Beads scatter. Clickers shout. This doesn't."

Ad: IMAGE 9:16, comparison (beads vs clicker vs ring bands), A1, ANG8, solution-aware, stage 3-4. Copy reviewed: 03-ads/ad_05/copy_final.md (identical to chiefed v2). Creative reviewed: report_media/ad_05_main.jpg (cream, ring band last), ad_05_img_v2.jpg (ring band first), ad_05_img_v3.jpg (dark green, side-by-side rows). Also read: CONTEXT.md, intake, user market research (incl. VOC table), avatar sheet, master avatar research, necessary beliefs, offer brief, brief.md, chief notes v1/v2, _assign.json entry, concept slate card C-02, the other 10 ads' copy_final.md, and the rebuilt product.html / index.html.

Bottom line: the previous chief approved this at "Tier 1, no residual concerns". That bar was mostly mechanical (word counts, banned-word scans). Read the way a senior DR copy chief with Islamic adab and Meta policy in mind would read it, this is a competent but generic comparison ad. The best element is the photo of the ring. The weakest elements are the second-person "you lost / you winced / weakness in you" lines, a hook whose payoff ("This doesn't.") has no antecedent, near-total overlap with ad_09, and a ring photo that appears to show the ring on the index/middle finger of a male-reading hand. Overall: 5.5/10 as written. It is fixable without changing the concept.

1. Hook strength (would A1 or A2 stop?)

How the hook works in the feed: the on-image headline and primary-text line 1 are the same words, so the hook is "Beads scatter. Clickers shout. This doesn't." plus a 3-band photo.

  • What works: three beats, parallel verbs, 6 words. "Shout" is a good, physical word for a loud clicker. The three objects are instantly familiar, and the viewer has owned at least one.
  • What does not: "This doesn't." has no concrete antecedent. It gets its meaning from the third band, which the thumb has to scroll past to read. In the main render, the headline carries no tribe marker. The words "dhikr", "tasbih", "istighfar", "salah" appear nowhere on the image. To a cold A1 it reads as a generic "counter ring vs. tally clicker" ad. The only faith cue is a string of wooden beads.
  • "Beads scatter" is not the A1 pain. Q29 is losing your place when interrupted (kids), not beads exploding across a desk. That happens only when a string snaps, which is rare. The image then shows a tidy bundle plus 5 loose beads, so the premise reads as staged. A fiqh/tasbih-literate commenter will say "my misbaha has never scattered".
  • A2 (gift buyer): nothing here speaks to them. The hook never names the category (a smart tasbih ring), and A2 is problem-aware, so it would not stop them. That is acceptable only if A2 is not a target here (brief says A1), so it is not a defect.

Ranking of the six existing hooks (best to worst, for A1 in a feed):

Rank Hook Why
1 H1 "Beads scatter. Clickers shout. This doesn't." Only one that fits the 3-band image, good rhythm. Weak as a standalone line and the "scatter" claim is exaggerated. Keep only if the image carries the product and a dhikr cue.
2 H4 "Clicker echoing in your quiet office?" Specific scene, situation-framed. "Your office" assumes a workplace but not a religion, so it is policy-safe. Strongest scroll-stop on its own. But it is also ad_09's and ad_11's territory.
3 H3 "One bump, and the count is gone." Vivid micro-scene, but the bump-scatter premise has the same accuracy problem as H1.
4 H6 "Beads drop. Clickers click. Rings tap." Rhymey. "Clickers click / rings tap" is a distinction without a difference to the reader.
5 H5 "Still losing count?" Generic. Duplicates ad_10 H5 "Lost count again?" and H1 "Lost your dhikr count?".
6 H2 "I lost my count on physical beads." First-person VOC reads as a customer testimonial (fabricated-endorsement risk per CONTEXT). Weak. Do not use.

2. Angle, avatar fit, awareness/sophistication, belief order

  • Angle (old tools fail) fits A1's real frictions (Q21 loud clicker, Q29 lost count). It is a legitimate angle.
  • Sophistication mismatch. The brief says stage 3-4, "mechanism stage, with copied mechanisms". Everyone claims lightweight/OLED/app/waterproof. The ad compares the ring only to beads and clickers, tools the A1 researcher has already moved past. A1 is comparing rings (iQibla, Hadiyah, clones) and fears "cheap clone dies in a month" (Q11, listed in this ad's own brief VOC and then not used anywhere). Nothing in the ad answers "why this ring and not another smart ring". Allowed differentiators that go unused: 1-year tech warranty, 60 days to try, the small display showing the count, IP67, app sync.
  • The mechanism is buried. The pulse at 33/66/99 is the one concrete mechanism and it appears only in the Meta headline and primary line 6. The original concept card had "Ring: tap. Pulse at 33." The final dropped it, leaving the winning band with the least specific label ("silent tap").
  • Belief order. Brief lists B4, B2. Copy installs B2 (L1, L4) then B4 (L5-6) — fine in that order. But:
  • B2 is installed by assertion ("Your intention was never the problem.", "That is friction in the tool, not weakness in you."), not by a scene. It does the opposite of what the research says to do (walk the failure in the avatar's own scene).
  • B4 "a ring can count silently, without a phone, noise or a look at a screen, so I can keep going wherever I am" is only half-installed: silent yes, "without a look at a screen / wherever I am" no. The pulse is what delivers "without looking down", and it is the least developed line.
  • B3 is deliberately skipped. Defensible given the brief, but see the faith/adab note in section 3: this comparison attacks beads, and the Sunnah-respect line is the guard against that.
  • B5/B6: offer line and 60-day guarantee are present. Fine.
  • N4 from the chief (an earned identity line for A1) was never applied. The ad has no moment (meeting, commute, bedside), only objects.

3. Rule violations (quote the line)

# Rule Offending line Severity Note
1 Meta personal attributes / "you" + negative self-perception "That is friction in the tool, not weakness in you." (primary L4) MUST Names a negative self-view ("weakness") and tells the viewer it is not theirs. Reads as addressing a perceived inner failing, and is itself a mild shame prompt ("no guilt or shame" rule). It also invents the problem it then relieves.
2 Meta personal attributes / asserts viewer experience and religious practice "You lost your count on physical beads when someone interrupted you." (L2) and "You winced at loud clicking in a quiet office space." (L3) MUST These are VOC paraphrases (Q29, Q21) rewritten as statements about the reader's past, with an invented emotion ("winced"). They presume the viewer is a bead user, a clicker user, and works in an office. They also assert religious practice by implication (dhikr counting). Convert to situation framing, not accusation.
3 Personal attributes (religion / inner state) "Your intention was never the problem." (L1) SHOULD (borderline) Asserts something about the viewer's niyyah. Faith-literate readers also bristle at anyone presuming to rule on intention. This exact line appears in 4 other ads (01, 04, 09, 10).
4 Fabricated-testimonial risk H2 "I lost my count on physical beads." (hook list only) SHOULD First-person, VOC-derived, no quote marks. Reads as a customer line. Not in the live copy, but drop it from the hook list.
5 Unconfirmed / over-claim On-image "Ring: silent tap" and "Clicker: everyone hears" SHOULD Brand wording is "silent tracking", never "completely silent". "Silent tap" implies the button makes no sound at all, which nobody has tested, and the commenters will ask "does the button click?". "Everyone hears" is an absolute for the clicker. Use "quiet" / "silent tracking".
6 Over-claim implied Headline "This doesn't." SHOULD Implies the ring never loses count and never fails. Defensible only if the count stays on the ring. "Works without your phone" and count persistence are [CONFIRM] items in CONTEXT; if any rewrite says "your count stays on the ring" or "no phone", tag it.
7 Men's finger Creative: all three renders show the hand with the ring on what appears to be the index/middle finger (v3 shows it clearly on the 2nd finger from the thumb side; main is the same hand, same position). The hand reads male (hair, build). MUST (flag for re-render) Per CONTEXT, copy must not direct men to the index finger and visuals showing a man doing so must be flagged. The rebuilt landing page FAQ also tells people "Most people wear it on the index finger", so the ad and landing page are aligned in the wrong direction. Re-render on the ring finger (or use an unambiguously female hand), and tell the owner the landing page copy needs the same fix.
8 Faith/adab: tone about beads "Beads scatter." / "Beads: count gone" SHOULD The misbaha is heritage, and the comment section knows fiqh. A flat "beads fail" us-vs-them tone invites "you're mocking the tasbih" and "the Sunnah is fingers" replies. The rebuilt landing page already says "A misbaha is beautiful, but..." and "Every tool has its place. This one goes everywhere.", a more adab-safe voice than the ad. The ad contradicts its own landing page tone.
9 Riya' Image shows "5332" on the ring display LOW Product-image consistency (same display across the campaign), not a brag, and no copy points at it. Fine. Just never turn it into "5,332 today" copy.
10 Faith accuracy / attribution / wudu / store metrics / superlatives / competitor names / sale price none PASS No Quran/hadith used, no wudu claim, no stats/stars, no named competitors, no sale price, no "waterproof"/IP68. This is clean and should be preserved.

Landing-page note for the owner (not ad copy): the rebuilt product.html and index.html still contain "8,200+ wearers", "4.9", named customer testimonials (Sarah W., Michael J., Ashley M., David B., Omar, Amara), and "Save $-49.99". Every ad in this run drives to those pages. Clicking through from an ad that carefully avoids them lands on the exact things the user ruled out, and invented testimonials are a Meta and FTC problem for the account regardless of what the ad says.

4. Copy-to-creative mismatch and on-image text

What the image shows (main render): cream background; headline top; three bands. (1) A tidy wooden bead strand with 5 loose beads and a frayed cord, labelled with a red X "Beads: count gone". (2) A metal hand tally clicker with three sound arcs, labelled "Clicker: everyone hears". (3) A hand on a laptop keyboard wearing the rose-gold ring, display reading 5332, labelled with a green check "Ring: silent tap". The bottom ~15% is empty cream.

Mismatches and problems: - Third band shows typing, not tapping. The hand is on the keys, the thumb is nowhere near the side button. The ring looks like jewelry; the thing the copy promises ("Tap to count", "gentle vibration at 33, 66 and 99") is not shown, and neither is any pulse. The visual shows "ring on a typing hand", not "ring counts". - Beads band does not show "count gone" or "scatter". It shows a mostly intact bracelet-style strand. The label has to do work the picture does not. - No dhikr/tasbih/Al Rayan cue anywhere on the image, not even the product name. The gold logo button is tiny. The ad does not say what the ring is for. - Right-hand/left-hand ambiguity and the finger issue (section 3 #7). - Dead space at the bottom, while the three labels are small and thin relative to the 9:16 frame. v3 (green) fixes some of this (stronger contrast and hierarchy) but the labels are still small. If a re-render is happening, v3's dark green is more on-brand for the audience and reads better at thumb size than the cream version. - Label quality: colon-fragments ("Beads: count gone", "Clicker: everyone hears", "Ring: silent tap") are tidy but flat. The third label is the weakest, the others show a failure and the third shows a vague virtue. The concept card's own "Ring: tap. Pulse at 33." was better. - Copy-to-image redundancy: primary L1 repeats the on-image headline word for word. The primary text should extend the image, not echo it.

Re-render needs (flag to the orchestrator): (a) ring on ring finger or neutral/female hand, (b) thumb on the button mid-tap, (c) add "dhikr" somewhere, (d) possibly new label 3 with the pulse, (e) fill or crop the bottom dead space. Text-only changes (headline/labels) still require a re-render, so only do them if the changes are the (c)/(d) ones, which are clearly worth it.

5. Clarity, rhythm, flab, clichés, repetition, overlap

Primary text, line by line:

Line Verdict
L1 "Beads scatter. Clickers shout. Your intention was never the problem." Duplicates the image headline, then adds the over-used "your intention" reframe.
L2 "You lost your count on physical beads when someone interrupted you." Accusatory "you" + past tense; flat, no scene; "physical beads" is a research artifact (people say "my beads").
L3 "You winced at loud clicking in a quiet office space." Invented emotion; "quiet office space" is VOC-21 residue.
L4 "That is friction in the tool, not weakness in you." "Friction" is marketing jargon, not the avatar's word (they say "tools"/"loud"/"lost count"). "Not X in you" cadence is the same AI-ish negation reframe used in 4 other ads. See violation #1.
L5 "The Al Rayan Dhikr Ring 2.0 sits on your finger. Tap to count your dhikr. Silent tracking, so there is no click to explain." Good, specific, correct claims. "No click to explain" is a nice human line, slightly implies concealing worship, fine.
L6 "A gentle vibration at 33, 66 and 99 tells you where you are. It looks like a ring." First sentence is the strongest line in the ad ("tells you where you are"). "It looks like a ring." is a non-sequitur dropped at the end, saying the least with the most air. Replace with a visible detail (small display showing the count).
L7-8 Offer + "Tap Shop Now and try it." Offer line correct. "Tap Shop Now" is the same CTA string in all 11 ads. "Try it" never connects to the 60 days in the same breath.

Meta headlines: 1. "Gentle vibrations at 33, 66 and 99" — correct claim, but a spec line, not a reason to click, and it detaches from the ad's angle (us-vs-them). It is also the line used in some form in all 11 ads. 2. "No click. No beads. Just tap." — closest to the angle; "No beads" is the knock again, and it is a near-duplicate of ad_09 H5 "No click. No beads." and ad_11 "No loud clicker. No beads to drop. No phone to open." 3. "$49.99. Free US shipping." — fine as a pure-offer alt.

Meta description "60-day money-back guarantee" duplicates primary L7.

Overlap with the other 10 (this is the biggest structural problem): - ad_09 is the same ad. "RETIRE THE LOUD CLICKER" + clicker vs ring image; primary text: "Loud clicking buttons in quiet office spaces. Beads you lose count on the second someone interrupts you. Your dhikr was never the problem. Your tools were." That is ad_05's L1-L4 almost line for line. Two feed ads with the same claim, same VOC, same offer, running to the same audience will compete and look repetitive. - "Your intention was never the problem / your tools were" appears in ad_01 (H3, body), ad_04 (H4, body), ad_09 (H3, body), ad_10 ("Your dhikr was never weak. Your tools made it hard."), ad_02 (H4). - Loud clicker as villain also in ad_06 (H3), ad_08 body, ad_11 H3 and body. - "Beads to drop" in ad_07 L2, ad_08, ad_09, ad_11. - "Tap. Pulse at 33, 66 and 99" is in every ad, so it cannot be this ad's differentiator by itself. - "Lost count" in ad_01, 02, 07, 10.

If ad_05 is to stay in the set it has to own something the others do not. Currently its only distinct feature is "three objects side by side".

MUST FIX

  1. Remove "weakness in you" (L4) and the accusatory second-person lines L2/L3 ("You lost your count... / You winced..."). Replace with scene or product framing that never tells the reader what they did, felt or are. Also drop "Your intention was never the problem." from this ad (used in 4 others, and a niyyah assertion).
  2. Visual: ring must not be shown on the index or middle finger of a male-reading hand (all three renders). Re-render on the ring finger, ideally mid-tap. Tell the owner the landing page FAQ ("Most people wear it on the index finger") needs the same fix.
  3. Break the near-duplicate with ad_09: change the claim, the villain order or the frame so the two ads are not the same ad.
  4. Primary text must not just echo the on-image headline in L1. Image headline and primary line 1 should do different jobs.
  5. Do not use H2 (first-person VOC) as a hook, and keep every VOC phrase out of quote marks and out of first person.
  6. "Ring: silent tap" -> brand wording ("silent tracking" / "quiet tap"). Any "count stays on the ring", "no phone needed", or battery/size-exchange line must carry a [CONFIRM] tag.

SHOULD FIX

  1. Put a dhikr/tasbih cue on the image (headline or label) and, if it can be done cheaply, the pulse at 33 on label 3 ("Ring: tap, pulse at 33").
  2. Show the tap (thumb on button) and, if possible, the pulse, not a typing hand.
  3. Soften the beads knock. "Beads scatter" is not the real pain (lost place when interrupted is) and invites misbaha-defence in comments. Either reframe to "where each tool works" or aim the criticism at the situation, not the heritage object.
  4. Replace "It looks like a ring." with something specific and visible (the small display shows the count / gold Al Rayan logo button).
  5. Use the unused VOC and benefits: Q11 (clone fear) is answered by 1-year tech warranty + 60-day money-back; Q32 (tech jewelry, not a toy) by the look; Q26 (size) by the 16-22 mm sizes.
  6. Replace the Meta headline with one that carries the angle or a concrete benefit and stays within 40 characters. "Gentle vibrations at 33, 66 and 99" is a spec.
  7. Drop "friction" as a word. The avatar says "tools", "loud", "lost count".
  8. Cut or recast "Tap Shop Now and try it." ("try it" should point at the 60 days) and make the description line say something different from primary L7.
  9. Fill the empty bottom 15% of the frame or crop the 9:16 so the labels can be larger.

KEEP (what works)

  • The three-object comparison idea. Instantly legible, and it is the format that gives this ad its own look versus the other ten.
  • The verbs "scatter"/"shout" as a rhythm (even if "scatter" needs a truer scene).
  • The ✓/✗ grammar and the ring-last placement (with the ring as the hero).
  • "Silent tracking, so there is no click to explain." (human, specific, brand-wording-safe).
  • "A gentle vibration at 33, 66 and 99 tells you where you are." — strongest sentence in the ad.
  • Clean offer line with only allowed facts ($49.99, free US shipping, 60-day money-back).
  • Absence of faith misquotes, metrics, stars, competitor names, sale price and "waterproof". Do not lose this.
  • Category-level objects only; finger counting is never shown or knocked.

Directions for the rewrite (3-5)

  1. Scene instead of accusation. Open the primary text with a situation the avatar recognises (a quiet room, the kids walking in mid-count, a clicker out on the desk) in neutral or "we hear this a lot" voice, never "You lost... / You winced...". Let the reader place themselves. Example lead: "Mid-count, the kids walk in. Where were you?" or "A quiet meeting. A clicker is not an option." No claims about the reader's inner state.
  2. Honest comparison frame, "every tool has its place". Beads are beautiful at home, the clicker is fine in a car, the ring is for the moments they cannot go. This matches the rebuilt landing page voice, respects the misbaha and the Sunnah (optional single line: "Fingers remain the Sunnah. The ring is for the long counts."), pre-empts the fiqh commenters, and clearly differs from ad_09's "RETIRE THE LOUD CLICKER" aggression.
  3. Make the ring band the answer to the sophisticated A1: tap, a small display showing the count, a pulse at 33/66/99 so you do not look down, and the "built to be kept" proof that is allowed (1-year tech warranty, 60-day money-back, $49.99). This answers Q11 (cheap clone dies) without naming a competitor.
  4. Fix the creative where it is clearly worth a re-render: ring on the ring finger, thumb on the button, a dhikr cue on the image, and the third label upgraded to "Ring: tap, pulse at 33" (or "quiet tap"). Keep text changes to those; do not redesign the layout. v3 (dark green) is the stronger base.
  5. Differentiate from the set: ad_05 owns "the three tools, side by side, honestly" (not "your tools are to blame" and not the 33/66/99 feature list). Use a short, plain primary text (about 5 lines), one CTA, one offer line, and a Meta headline that names a benefit or the comparison ("Beads, clicker or ring?" style) rather than a spec. Avoid the shared phrases "your intention", "your dhikr was never weak", "no beads to drop" and "Tap Shop Now".

A ring. Or a tasbih?

video, "ring or tasbih" meme inversion

Primary text paste-ready

A ring, or a tasbih? Depends who's looking.

Al Rayan Dhikr Ring 2.0: across a meeting table, a plain band. Up close, a small display with your count.

Tap to count dhikr. A gentle vibration marks 33, 66 and 99. Silent tracking, so the meeting carries on.

Dhikr is much older than any ring. The Prophet ﷺ said: "Let not your tongue cease to be moist with the remembrance of Allah." (Tirmidhi 3375)

Finger counting remains the Sunnah. The ring is a helper for the days a count runs long or your hands are full.

$49.99. Free US shipping. 60-day money-back guarantee.
Shop Now.

Meta headlines

  1. Ring or tasbih? Look closer. (28) ← recommended
  2. Dhikr in the quiet gaps (23)
  3. Count dhikr. The meeting carries on. (36)

Meta description · CTA

60-day money-back guarantee (27)

  • Button: Shop Now
  • VO close: "Sixty-day money-back." The pill carries FREE US SHIPPING. The button carries the action, so "Tap Shop Now" is not read aloud.
  • Offer: $49.99 · free US shipping · 60-day money-back guarantee. No sale price. (1-year tech warranty is available as an alternate line.)

Hooks

ID Type (slug) Hook (exact) Overlay / pill text

H2 pattern-inversion A plain band across the table. A tasbih up close. WHAT IT ACTUALLY IS
H3 POV caption POV: the ring in this meeting is a tasbih. POV: THE MEETING CARRIES ON
H4 situation The meeting runs long. The dhikr doesn't have to stop. LONG MEETING, 3 PM
H5 brand statement A smart tasbih you wear like a ring. RING OR TASBIH?
H6 question Guess what this ring is counting. NOT THE MINUTES

Recommended: H1. Five words that fit the 2.05 s window, "tasbih" as the in-group cue in the first second, a question the next clip answers, no first person. H2 is the strongest static or feed-caption alternative. H6 is the funniest but needs a caption-layer overlay that competes with the baked pill. H4 is for primary-text or test use only.

SCRIPT

Voice: warm, dry brand narrator (female, calm, close-mic). Pace about 2.3 w/s with a breath at each cut. Pronunciation: tasbih = "tas-BEEH", dhikr = "THIK-r". Verify both by ear on the re-record (the v1 TTS mispronounced tasbih).

That is why the copy says "a ring" and "up close", and never "nobody can tell" or "nobody notices". Do not extend the claim.

Ad info: angle, avatar, beliefs
  • Format: video, 9:16, 3 existing clips, 11.3 s (C01 0.0-2.1 · C02 2.1-6.8 · C03 6.8-11.3)
  • Concept: swipe:ergosalah-71735717 (what the room sees vs what it actually is), kept
  • Angle: ANG6/ANG1, narrowed to "how the room reads the ring". Territory for this ad: the glance across the table, the small joke, the private smile. Tool-friction stories (clicker, beads, phone) and the 33/66/99 pulse demo belong to other ads and are not used in the VO.
  • Avatar: A1 discreet daily rememberer. The viewer is never addressed as "Muslim" and no line asserts a practice. The words stay situational.
  • Awareness: solution-aware · Sophistication: stage 3-4
  • Framework: unique-mechanism as one plain verb chain: tap, count, silent tracking
  • Voice: brand narrator only (female, calm, close-mic), not the woman on screen. No "I", "my", "me" in VO, captions or primary text.

Editor notes: changes, checks, re-render, owner to confirm

Editor notes

What I changed from the rewrite, and why

  1. L2 line 3: "Feel a pulse at thirty-three" became "Silent tracking." Three reasons. (a) At 1.7 s of window it was 6 words, about 3.5 w/s, too fast to land. (b) ad_07 owns the "pulse at 33" demo (its H5, headline and VO), so this would have been a near-duplicate line across two video ads. (c) Over the shipped picture, which shows a glow on the band, "pulse" invites a light-equals-vibration misread. "Silent tracking" is the brand's own wording and fits this ad's whole premise (the room hears nothing). The 33, 66 and 99 vibration stays in the primary text.
  2. L2 re-timed to the measured picture. The other finger arrives at about 3.65 s and touches at about 4.4-4.7 s, with the glow running from about 4.7 s. The rewrite put "Tap to count dhikr" at 3.2-4.5, before the finger appeared. Now "Tap" starts at 3.45 and "dhikr" lands on the touch.
  3. L3 trimmed and re-timed. The rewrite spoke "Free US shipping. Sixty-day money-back." (about 5 tokens that are 11 syllables) in 2.2 s, which is a rushed close. VO now says only the guarantee; the baked pill carries free shipping, so each offer is stated once and neither contradicts the other. The payoff line now runs 7.25-9.00 into the smile, which I measured as starting at about 8.75 s (the rewrite assumed 8.0-9.4).
  4. Hooks H2, H4, H5 replaced. Old H2 "Looks like a ring. Counts dhikr." was a near-copy of ad_11's alt hook "Looks like a ring. Counts like a tasbih." Old H4 "Your dhikr doesn't have to stop" asserted the viewer's practice (borderline personal attribute); now "The dhikr". Old H5 "passes for jewellery" implied concealment; now "you wear like a ring".
  5. Primary text rebuilt for uniqueness and compliance. New opener; product name moved inside the first 125 characters; the hadith gets a lead-in ("Dhikr is much older than any ring") so it cannot read as the ring fulfilling the hadith; the Sunnah line is reworded because "Fingers stay the Sunnah. The ring is for the long counts." is verbatim ad_09's H5 and close to ad_05, ad_07 and ad_11. "Across a meeting table, it looks like a ring" sat too close to ad_11's "In the waiting room, it just looks like a ring", so the line now frames the glance and the display up close.
  6. Burned captions re-chunked to 14 characters or fewer; the rewrite's "Tap to count dhikr", "Dhikr in the quiet gaps" and "Sixty-day money-back" would wrap to two lines at 720 px width.
  7. Meta description cut to "60-day money-back guarantee" (27) so it neither repeats the headline nor the pill, and stays short for the link-description slot.
  8. Caption position: kept the rewrite's MarginV 540 for C01/C03 but dropped the change for C02, so the caption does not jump around the finger and glow zone.

Verification done

  • Hadith: Tirmidhi 3375 checked word for word against offer_brief.md row 7: "Let not your tongue cease to be moist with the remembrance of Allah." Attributed to "The Prophet ﷺ", which is the Prophet's own saying in that narration. No other Quran or hadith is used. No Tirmidhi 3583 or Muslim 783.
  • No claim that the ring earns reward, raises iman or gives khushu'; reward is not mentioned at all. Finger counting is honoured and the ring is not placed above it.
  • 33/66/99 is stated as three vibration points and never tied to a 100th count.
  • No first person in VO, captions or primary text. No "Muslim at work", no viewer religion, no "you" plus a negative self-perception. "Your count", "your hands" are about the object, not an assertion about the viewer.
  • No store metrics, stars, reviews, testimonials, superlatives, competitor mentions, sale price, delivery speed, weight, IP68, "waterproof", "completely silent", "nobody notices". Silent tracking is used as brand wording.
  • Wudu, men's-finger and rose-gold rules: not touched. On-screen wearer is a woman, ring on the ring finger, black band.
  • All claims used are in "Allowed product claims", so no [CONFIRM] tags are needed: tap to count, small display with the count, vibration at 33, 66 and 99, silent tracking, $49.99, free US shipping, 60-day money-back guarantee.
  • Meta headline lengths: 28, 23, 36 (all <=40). Description 27.
  • Duplication check against the other rewrites and finals (ad_01-ad_11): no shared hook or headline. The deliberate shared elements across the batch are the standard price/guarantee line and the allowed vibration wording.

Re-render needed

  • VO re-record: yes (all three lines are new). Update captions.ass with the table above.
  • Clip re-render: none required. Optional C02 re-roll is described under "Clip change notes"; the C01 and C03 pill swaps can be done as assembly patches without re-rendering.

Owner to confirm

  • No [CONFIRM] claims are used in this ad.
  • Pronunciation of "tasbih" (tas-BEEH) and "dhikr" (THIK-r) on the re-recorded VO.
  • Whether the baked first-person pill "WHAT MY COWORKERS SEE" is acceptable, or should be patched to "WHAT THE ROOM SEES".
  • Whether to re-roll C02 to remove the glow and fix the garbled display digits.
  • Landing page check before launch: a grep of the rebuilt product.html and index.html found no "4.9", "8,200" or named testimonial text, but the critique reported them on an earlier version of the pages. Recheck the live pages, since this ad sends traffic there.
Critique of the previous version

ad_06 critique (video, "what my coworkers see vs what it is")

Role: copy critic. Not a rewrite. Reviewed: 03-ads/ad_06/copy_final.md, brief.md, chief notes, 04-assets/ad_06/script_display.txt, prompts/vo.txt, captions.ass, captions.words.json (real VO timings), clip_args.txt, the 3 start frames, final.mp4 (1 fps contact sheet), the swipe analysis (ErgoSalah 71735717), the other 10 ads' copy_final.md, and the rebuilt store pages.

Verdict in one line: the concept (meme inversion, hijabi woman in a meeting, ring as camouflage) is sound and the copy is clean on faith and metrics. But the hook is soft, the script is a feature list with a coupon at the end, the on-screen text and the VO disagree in two places, the first-person voice is a testimonial risk, and the angle is now nearly a duplicate of ad_11.

0. What actually shipped (so the critique is anchored)

WHAT IT ACTUALLY IS Lap-height shot, ring now facing camera with the display lit. Her OTHER hand's index finger comes in and pokes the ring once; a white flare appears on the band. No face.
C03 6.8-11.3 "Sixty days, money back." 7.32-9.00 / gap / "Tap Shop Now." 10.04-10.78 Sixty days money back / Tap Shop Now FREE US SHIPPING Same shot as C01, ring now facing camera, she gives a small private smile.

Notes: copy_final.md labels L1 "ON-CAM" and times it 0.0-3.4. In the render it is VO over a closed mouth, and the cut to C02 is at 2.1. Total 21 words over 11.3 s = 1.86 w/s overall, with about 3.9 s of dead air (0.6 s, 0.8 s, 1.0 s, 1.0 s gaps plus the tail).

1. Hook strength and ranking

First second as shipped: a calm woman at a table, a colleague glance, a green pill, and a fast VO line. There is no movement, no tension, no visible object of interest (the ring is a plain black band with its display turned away, so there is literally nothing to look at), and no in-group flag in words. The swipe's hook (ErgoSalah) worked because the caption named the tribe ("What muslim think i sell" plus emoji) and the contrast was funny (🤢💀 vs 🥺☁️). Ours is a mild contrast (a person fidgeting vs a tasbih) with no word that tells the A1 scroller "this is for me". "Fidgeting" also invites the wrong read: a fidget-ring product.

Would A1/A2 stop? A1: maybe 50/50, mainly because of the hijab and "tasbih" arriving at second 2.4. A2 (gift buyer): no, nothing signals product category or gifting. The line is passive, observational, and gives no reason to stay for the reveal.

Ranking of the six hooks: 1. H5 "Guess what I'm counting in this meeting." / NOT THE MINUTES. Best curiosity gap, ties straight to the scene, the overlay joke is the funniest line in the document, and the reveal ("dhikr") is a real payoff. Risk: first person (see section 3). Reframe as POV caption or brand voice. 2. H1 "Coworkers think I'm fidgeting with a ring." The right swipe structure and a real moment (everyone has been the person with something in their hands in a meeting). Weakness: no in-group word, no stakes, rushed delivery. Needs a sharper first noun. 3. H3 "Loud clicker. Quiet office. Everyone staring." Strong VOC-21 pain and rhythm, but it is the ad_09 and ad_05 territory and nothing in the clips shows a clicker, so it would mismatch the picture. 4. H6 "Modern tech jewelry, not a cheap plastic toy." This is a lightly reworded Q32 review line turned into a brand claim, and "not a cheap plastic toy" implies a competitor comparison. Weak hook, no scene. 5. H2 "I was afraid a tasbih ring would look ridiculous at work." Q40 is a review of a different product, lifted into first person. Reads as a testimonial. Opening with a fear is also on the shaming side. 6. H4 "Muslim at work? Your tasbih can look like a ring." / FOR MUSLIMS AT WORK. Disqualified: asserts religion of the viewer (Meta personal-attributes) and presumes the viewer owns a tasbih.

2. Angle, avatar, awareness, belief order

  • Angle fit: good. "Looks like an ordinary ring, works as a silent tasbih" is on-brief for A1 and backed by VOC-40/22/21 and the store ("To everyone else it's simply a ring").
  • Awareness/sophistication: solution-aware, stage 3-4. The script skips straight to the product, which is right for A1, but at stage 3-4 the market has heard "silent, vibration, app, IP67" from every competitor. The script's only mechanism is those two feature words. It lacks a specific moment/mechanism (what the tap does, what you feel at 33) and an identification beat (why this matters in this room).
  • B4 is asserted, not demonstrated. B4 = "a ring I wear can count silently, without a phone, noise or a look at a screen". The VO says "silent tracking, gentle vibration": two nouns, no verb, no benefit. The picture never shows a count rising (the display digits in the AI render are garbled/mirrored and not legible, roughly "2EE5" instead of 5332), no phone-free, no "you don't need to look". The pulse is shown as a bright white lens-flare on the band, which is not how a vibration works.
  • Beat B4 -> B6 is a jump. B6 ("trying it is low-risk") is installed by a bare "Sixty days, money back." with no price, no reason to want it, and no payoff for the emotion the last clip is built around (her private smile). The brief's own optional faith payoff ("keep your tongue moist") was never used in the video, only in primary text. The emotional peak of the creative (C03, the half-smile) is wasted on an offer line.
  • "Tasbih" is the only identity word and it is never explained ("It's my tasbih"). Fine for A1, opaque for A2, and "dhikr" never appears in VO, captions or pills. The ad never says what the ring does for a tasbih in plain words: it counts.
  • "Tracking" is the wrong verb for dhikr audiences. The user doc itself says dhikr "was never a score". "Silent tracking" is allowed brand wording but "count" is warmer and the verb the avatar uses.

3. Rule violations and risks (quoted)

# Line / asset Rule Severity
1 H4 "Muslim at work? Your tasbih can look like a ring." and overlay "FOR MUSLIMS AT WORK" Meta personal attributes (asserts the viewer's religion), plus presumes viewer owns a tasbih. MUST (do not carry into rewrite)
2 L1 "Coworkers think I'm fidgeting with a ring." / L2 "It's my tasbih." / primary text "It's my tasbih, counting dhikr through the whole meeting." First-person story voiced by an AI persona = reads as a customer endorsement; there is no verified Al Rayan customer. CONTEXT allows only (a) brand voice, (b) clearly relatable second-person scenario, (c) founder line. The brief's "AI UGC personas may speak first person" is overridden by the round rules. MUST
3 H2 "I was afraid a tasbih ring would look ridiculous at work." A near-verbatim Q40 (a review of a different product) rephrased as a personal confession. Fabricated-testimonial risk. MUST (drop or recast)
4 H6 "Modern tech jewelry, not a cheap plastic toy." Q32 review language used as brand claim; implied attack on other products. SHOULD
5 Claim of the whole ad: "Coworkers think it's just a ring." with C03 showing the lit display facing the camera right next to a pen, and the store/product images showing an obvious digital display and gold button The central claim ("passes as a regular ring", from a VOC about another ring) is only true in C01 because the display was deliberately turned to the palm side. Unconfirmed that it is worn that way. Mild misleading-depiction risk; also invites "nobody will ever know", which is a stronger claim than we have. SHOULD (soften to "looks like jewellery / a ring", never "nobody can tell")
6 C02 flare on the ring (a white light burst at 33) Product has no light effect; vibration is haptic. Visual misrepresents the product feature. Copy "gentle vibration" over a flash is a mismatch. SHOULD (clip change to a subtle display glow or no flash; or caption honestly "felt, not seen")
7 Primary text: "counting dhikr through the whole meeting." Adab and employer-trust read: dhikr "through the whole meeting" frames her as mentally elsewhere while a colleague speaks. In the visuals she is attentive, so the line over-claims. Comment-section audience will notice. SHOULD ("between points", "in the quiet gaps", "while the meeting carries on")
8 Primary text: hadith placed directly after the product description Tirmidhi 3375 wording is exact and cited (OK). But placed straight after "This one you wear" it can read as the ring fulfilling the hadith. Attribution should stay "The Prophet ﷺ said". Needs the Sunnah/finger-counting note nowhere else in the ad, since the ad is silent on it. SHOULD (reorder; add the "fingers remain Sunnah" line only if space)
9 Primary text "A gentle vibration at 33, 66 and 99." OK. Does not imply 100. pass
10 Primary text "Loud clickers and phone apps were never made for a quiet office." Not an attack on named competitors, but a flat generalisation about apps. Also the "blame your tools" line this round must avoid repeating everywhere. SHOULD (overlap, see section 5)
11 Wudu, men's finger, rose gold, riya', store metrics, superlatives, sale price None present. The only visual is a woman on the ring finger. pass
12 Landing-page congruence (not our copy, but the ad sends traffic there) Rebuilt store pages contain "4.9", "8,200+ wearers" and named testimonials (Sarah W., Michael J., ...), which the user banned and which are unverified. Our ad avoids them, but the owner should know the page it lands on breaks the user's rule. FYI to owner

Claims tagged: "silent tracking" is brand wording (OK). Anything about working without a phone, battery, or free size exchange: not used here. Keep it that way, or tag [CONFIRM].

4. Copy-to-creative mismatch and on-image text

  1. Pill 3 "FREE US SHIPPING" vs VO "Sixty days, money back." The pill is on screen for the full 4.5 s of C03 while the VO says a different offer. Free shipping is never spoken, and the guarantee is never on screen except as a caption. Two offers, no hierarchy. (Pill is baked into the clip start frame, so changing it means a clip change.)
  2. Pill 1 + caption + VO say the same thing three times ("WHAT MY COWORKERS SEE", "Coworkers think I'm", "Coworkers think I'm fidgeting..."). Zero information added after the first word. Burned captions in C01 also sit directly on the hand and ring (the picture composes the hand low, around 70-85% of frame height, not at the 46-54% in the prompt), so the caption covers the subject of the shot.
  3. Pill 2 "VS... WHAT IT ACTUALLY IS" does not say what it is. The reveal is then the VO "It's my tasbih", with no mention of dhikr or counting. A hard-cut reveal that withholds the payoff noun.
  4. Hook text says "fidgeting", the picture shows her perfectly still, tapping a pen once. The "coworker unbothered/approving nod" in the prompt also undermines the comedy: if he is not puzzled, there is nothing to invert.
  5. The count never visibly rises in C02, and the digits are garbled/mirrored. B4 and "tap to count" rely on the audience believing it.
  6. Tap mechanism shown is the other hand's index finger poking the ring. The store describes a side button pressed with the thumb of the same hand. The shipped clip (c02_a2) was a fallback; it looks like a two-handed gesture in a "discreet" ad.
  7. C03: her mouth is slightly open in the first frame (t=7) while the VO is not in sync, then she smiles. Reads as lip-sync noise. The smile is an emotional peak with no line under it.
  8. Pronunciation: the brief says "TAZ-bee", the TTS prompt used "tas-beeh". Whisper confidence on "tasbih" was 0.09. Verify by ear; "tas-BEEH" is the community pronunciation.
  9. Typography: pills are clean, correctly spelled, high contrast (good, keeps meme feel). Burned captions are Arial Black white with shadow, readable. Fine.

5. Clarity, rhythm, flab, overlap

  • Rhythm: three separate mini-statements with silence between each, so it plays as slides, not as a flow. L2 is two nouns. L3 is a coupon read aloud ("Tap Shop Now" is an imperative with no reason).
  • "Tap Shop Now" appears in the VO, the caption, and the Meta button. Same words in all 10 ads. Cliché, and in-video it is not clickable.
  • Primary text: five lines, all generic: "Loud clickers and phone apps were never made for a quiet office." duplicates ad_09 ("Retire the loud clicker. It was never built for a quiet office") and ad_05; "Tap to count. A small display shows where you are. A gentle vibration at 33, 66 and 99." is a verbatim twin of ad_11, ad_10, ad_05, ad_07.
  • Direct overlap with ad_11 (image): ad_11 hook "A ring with a hidden tasbih", ad_11 primary "It looks like a ring. Under the table, it counts your dhikr.", ad_11 headline "A tasbih hidden on your finger" vs ad_06 "Coworkers think it's just a ring. It's my tasbih, counting dhikr..." and headline "A tasbih that looks like a ring". Same angle, same scene (office/under the table), same reveal. Also overlaps ad_03 ("It looks like a ring, not a gadget"), ad_02 ("A discreet tool you can have all the time at work"), ad_09/ad_05 (quiet office, loud clicker). ad_06 needs its own territory: the social moment and the human reaction (how others read you), humour, the private smile. Not the feature.
  • Headlines: "A tasbih that looks like a ring" (31) is a near duplicate of ad_11's. "Silent dhikr, even in meetings" is better but still generic. "Dhikr ring. 60-day money-back" is a label. The Meta description duplicates the headline 3.
  • Flab: "WHAT MY COWORKERS SEE" repeated; "gentle vibration" without purpose; "through the whole meeting"; the primary text could lose lines 2 and 3 entirely.

6. Script timing

Real VO density: 21 words in 11.3 s, but L1 is delivered at 3.9 w/s (cramped hook) while the middle and end have 1-second gaps. The line windows that the clips actually give you:

Clip Window Capacity at 2.3-2.6 w/s Used now Problem
C01 0.0-2.1 s about 5 words 7 Crammed; the hook is read too fast to land.
C02 2.1-6.8 s about 10-11 7 (3 + 4 with a 0.8 s gap) Under-filled; two separate fragments with air in between.
C03 6.8-11.3 s about 10 7 (4 + 3 with 1 s gap; starts 0.5 s late, ends 0.5 s early) The smile has no line; offer fragmented.

Timing/visual alignment: "It's my tasbih" (2.36-3.76) lands as the macro appears: good. "Silent tracking" (4.5-5.3) lines up with the poke; "gentle vibration" (5.7-6.4) with the flare: matches, but the flare comes after the finger has left, so the cause-effect is muddy. The first 0.5 s of C03 has no VO. The offer lands over a picture of her smiling at a speaker: no visual support for "money back".

MUST FIX

  1. Remove first-person testimony voice (L1, L2, primary text line 1/2, H2, H5 as "I"): rewrite as brand voice, a second-person relatable scenario, or POV captions. No "my tasbih".
  2. Delete H4 and "FOR MUSLIMS AT WORK"; do not recreate any "Muslim at work / as a Muslim" framing. Prefer situation framing ("In the meeting"), not identity.
  3. Fix the hook: first noun must carry tension and an in-group cue within about 2 s; speak it at a pace that lands (at most 5 words in 2.1 s).
  4. Make the VO and on-screen offer agree (pill says FREE US SHIPPING, VO says 60 days). Either speak both in a clean order or request a pill change as a clip change.
  5. Name the payoff in the reveal: say "dhikr" or "counts" in the VO or caption. Do not rely on "tasbih" alone.
  6. Stop depicting a non-existent light flash as the vibration; fix wording or flag a clip change. Do not claim "nobody can tell / nobody notices".

SHOULD FIX

  • Replace "tracking" with "count" wherever the brand-wording constraint allows (keep "silent tracking" only where needed).
  • Rework "counting dhikr through the whole meeting" (adab and professionalism).
  • Give C03's smile a line; end on a human beat, then the offer. Add price ($49.99) or free shipping consistently, not both fragmentary.
  • Replace "Tap Shop Now" in the VO with a reason or drop it from the VO (keep the Meta button).
  • Differentiate the headlines and primary text from ad_11, ad_09 and ad_05; remove the verbatim "Tap to count. A small display shows where you are..." paragraph.
  • Move burned captions off the ring and hand in C01 and C03.
  • Cut the hadith from where it implies the ring fulfils it, or give it a one-line lead-in, keep exact wording and "The Prophet ﷺ said" attribution.
  • Verify "tasbih" pronunciation in the audio.
  • Flag the store testimonials and 8,200+ metrics to the owner.

KEEP (what works)

  • The inversion structure and the fact that there is a person, a room and a reaction (the colleague glance) in the first frame.
  • The hijabi persona and the modest, believable office; woman on the ring finger (no men's finger issue).
  • Pill typography and the meme device: clean, spelled right, high contrast. The Q40 idea itself ("passes as a ring") is the right desire.
  • "It's my tasbih." as a three-word reveal: short, in-group, lands as the macro appears.
  • "Silent tracking" and "60 days money back" are accurate to approved claims; no store metrics, no superlatives, no wudu or reward claims.
  • The private-smile close in C03 is the best emotional beat in the asset; build the final line around it.
  • Tirmidhi 3375 wording is exact (just reposition).

Directions for the rewrite

  1. Own the "how others read you" space (differentiate from ad_11). Keep the meme, make the humour sharper: the coworker's reading of the ring vs the quiet truth. First line should name the scene and tension, e.g. a question that makes the viewer guess what the ring is doing ("Guess what this ring is counting."), with the pill carrying "WHAT MY COWORKERS SEE". Never "my".
  2. Brand-voice or POV, not testimony. Use captions in a POV register ("POV: the ring in this meeting is a tasbih") or a brand narrator ("It looks like a ring. It counts dhikr."), or second-person scenario ("You think it's a ring..."), so no AI person claims an experience.
  3. Spend the C02 window (4.7 s, about 10 words) on one connected sentence that says what the ring does in plain verbs: tap, count, feels at 33/66/99, no sound, no screen to open. One sentence, not two noun fragments. Keep "silent tracking" only if exact brand wording is needed.
  4. Give the C03 smile its line, then the offer. A short human payoff (keeping the tongue moist / dhikr between points, no guilt, no reward claim), then one offer line: $49.99 plus 60-day money-back, with free shipping handled once (pill or VO, not both competing).
  5. Rework the primary text from scratch: scene-first, one idea, no ad_09/ad_11 phrases, one CTA, hadith (exact, attributed to the Prophet ﷺ) positioned as the human reason, not as the ring's promise; write three headlines that do not echo "a tasbih that looks like a ring". Note any clip changes explicitly: pill 3 text, the flare in C02, the digits on the display, and (optional) a thumb-tap version of C02.

Primary text paste-ready

Was that thirty-three, or thirty-four?

Counting on the fingers is the Sunnah. The ring is for the moment the number slips mid-tasbih. Tap to count, and the display shows your number.

The Prophet ﷺ said (narrated by Ka'b b. 'Ujra): "...the repeaters of which after every prescribed prayer will never be caused disappointment: 'Glory be to Allah' thirty-three times, 'Praise be to Allah' thirty-three times, and 'Allah is most Great' thirty-four times." (Sahih Muslim 596a)

The ring only counts. It pulses gently at 33, 66 and 99. The 100th is yours to say. Silent tracking.

Al Rayan Dhikr Ring 2.0: $49.99, free US shipping, 60-day money-back guarantee, 1-year tech warranty. Tap Shop Now.
